**Front:** How was power balanced among the regional states before Mughal expansion?
**Definition (learn):** The fifteenth-century pattern of mutual checks in which rivals limited each other rather than fighting a single struggle.
**Breakdown:**
- West: Gujarat, Malwa and Mewar checked one another (✅ owner 08 §1).
- East: Bengal checked by Orissa and Jaunpur; Lodis later fought Jaunpur for the Ganga–Yamuna valley (✅ owner 08 §1).
- Malwa's plateau made it a barrier between Gujarat, Mewar, Bahmanis and Delhi (✅ owner 08 §1).
- Conflicts rarely crossed regional limits; a structured balance emerged (✅ adv. 08 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Balance of power before 1526 (⚠️ year framing by analyst).
**Links:** MED-2001, MED-2014, MED-2016, MED-2023.
**Trap:** A balance is not a "Hindu vs Muslim" binary. Advanced owner: "Source shows alliances and rivalries were often regional, strategic and commercial" (✅ adv. 08 §5).
**Mains hook:** The Gujarat–Malwa–Mewar–Jaunpur–Bengal equilibrium delayed any single power from unifying north India until the Mughals disrupted it (✅ owner 08 §8.1).
**Back:** *"West: three check three. East: Bengal held by Orissa and Jaunpur."*

### Flashcard MED-2023 — Comparative state-capacity matrix (five states)
**Front:** Compare the capital, material base and cultural idiom of Bengal, Gujarat, Malwa, Jaunpur and Kashmir.
**Definition (learn):** A comparison table linking political geography to economic base and cultural expression.
**Breakdown:**
- Bengal — Pandua/Gaur, delta routes, agrarian surplus, textiles, ports; Persianate-Bengali synthesis (✅ owner 08 §17).
- Gujarat — Ahmedabad/Champaner, port hinterland, commerce and crafts; Persianate with Jain/Hindu craft (✅ owner 08 §17).
- Malwa — Mandu plateau, routes and forts; contested court (✅ owner 08 §17).
- Jaunpur — Ganga valley, revenue and towns; Sharqi sovereignty and learning (✅ owner 08 §17).
- Kashmir — valley court, agriculture and crafts; Persianate-Sanskrit-Kashmiri/Rishi-Sufi interaction (✅ owner 08 §17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** The matrix is an analytical summary from the owner (✅ owner 08 §17).
**Links:** MED-2001, MED-2006, MED-2022, MED-2024 (Ahom contrast).
**Trap:** "No state was merely a miniature Delhi Sultanate" (✅ owner 08 §17). Do not claim that any regional state copied the Delhi model unchanged.
**Mains hook:** Answer 15–20 marks by moving from capital and material base to idiom and inter-state pressure (✅ owner 08 §21).
**Back:** *"Capital, base, idiom: five states, five answers, no copies."*

### Flashcard MED-2024 — Ahom/Assam (bounded extension): state formation and paik system
**Front:** What political-administrative facts about the Ahom polity matter for the Prelims extension?
**Definition (learn):** A Brahmaputra-valley polity that developed outside the Delhi-centred system, with the paik organisation of rotational labour and military service.
**Breakdown:**
- Sukapha entered the Brahmaputra valley; the owner dates this to 1228 traditionally (⚠️ owner basic states "thirteenth century"; owner §16.1 gives 1228).
- Paik system: adult male subjects owed rotational labour/military service (⚠️ owner; not chattel slavery, adv. 08 §2).
- Buranjis: Ahom chronicles, a key source for political and social history (⚠️ owner; not neutral, adv. 08 §5 / §16.2).
- Ahom identity and state built through interaction, not isolation (✅ owner 08 §16.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sukapha ~1228 (owner §16.1). ⚠️ Wikipedia (Charaideo article) gives the founding of Charaideo as 1253 CE by Sukaphaa; this conflicts with the owner's 1228 entry and is flagged, not resolved. Paik is a service obligation, not generic forced labour (✅ owner 08 §2).
**Links:** MED-2025 (Moidams), MED-2037 (source criticism), MED-2023 (matrix).
**Trap:** Do not reduce paik to chattel slavery; do not treat Buranjis as neutral contemporaneous records.
**Mains hook:** Ahom shows medieval state formation beyond the Sultanate system; use it as a counterpoint to the Delhi-centred narrative.
**Back:** *"Sukapha, paik, Buranji: Brahmaputra's own state, not Delhi's copy."*

### Flashcard MED-2025 — Moidams of Charaideo (Tai-Ahom royal burial mounds)
**Front:** What are the Moidams of Charaideo and what is their boundary with Art & Culture?
**Definition (learn):** Tai-Ahom royal mound burials at Charaideo, described by the owner's UNESCO reference as created across the thirteenth to nineteenth centuries.
**Breakdown:**
- Royal funerary landscape; mound burials of Ahom royalty (✅ wiki: Charaideo article).
- Political-administrative context belongs to Medieval History; form, cosmology, World Heritage status and conservation belong to Art & Culture (✅ owner 08 §2, §16.2).
- Date range: owner states 13th–19th century (⚠️ not confirmed by the fetched Wikipedia article).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Inscribed on the World Heritage List on 26 July 2024 (✅ wiki: Charaideo article). Owner gives a 13th–19th century range; the fetched Wikipedia article did not confirm the range (⚠️). Official UNESCO pages could not be fetched.
**Links:** MED-2024, MED-2030 (PYQ route).
**Trap:** Moidams = funerary landscape; Buranji = chronicle. Do not present Moidams as a Medieval Indian political-administrative institution.
**Mains hook:** Use Moidams only to show that burial architecture encodes royal legitimacy; leave cosmology and conservation to Art & Culture.
**Back:** *"Moidam holds the king, Buranji tells the story."*

### Flashcard MED-2026 — Trap: regional states were not subordinate, and not simply decline
**Front:** What is the single most important trap in regional kingdom questions?
**Definition (learn):** Two linked errors: treating regional states as subordinate to Delhi after 1338, and treating them as mere residue of Delhi's decline.
**Breakdown:**
- Bengal "remained subordinate to Delhi after Firuz Tughlaq's campaigns" is wrong; it exchanged gifts but was not subordinate (✅ owner 08 §5).
- Regional kingdoms are not "decline" stories: many were centres of prosperity and cultural synthesis (✅ owner 08 §5 trap box).
- Malwa was not important only culturally; its geography was central to north Indian power politics (✅ owner 08 §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Firuz Tughlaq's Bengal campaign (owner-cited; date ⚠️ not in this file).
**Links:** MED-2001, MED-2002, MED-2014, MED-2022.
**Trap:** The answer to both errors is "autonomous polities with real but contested authority".
**Mains hook:** "Regional kingdoms were not symptoms of decline but autonomous polities" (✅ owner 08 §8.1).
**Back:** *"Not subordinate, not residue: autonomous, with a border."*

### Flashcard MED-2027 — PYQ route: 2022 Prelims Q92 (ruler–dynasty pairs)
**Front:** Which PYQ routes to Topic 08, and what is the answer status?
**Definition (learn):** 2022 Prelims GS-I Q92 asks for early medieval king and dynasty pairs; this is a direct Topic-08 route.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: ruler–dynasty pair identification (✅ owner 08 §20.1 and PYQ integration).
- Answer status: official key unavailable locally; no answer recorded (✅ owner 08 §20.1).
- Practice: match each regional ruler to his state (Bengal, Gujarat, Malwa, Jaunpur, Kashmir) using the chronology ladders above.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Prelims GS-I Q92 (✅ owner 08 PYQ block).
**Links:** MED-2012, MED-2016, MED-2018, MED-2025.
**Trap:** Do not invent the key for this question; use the ruler–state pairs from this file.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable; this is a Prelims pair route.
**Back:** *"Ninety-two asks pairs: ruler, dynasty, state — pair them, don't guess."*

### Flashcard MED-2028 — PYQ route: 2023 Prelims Q49 (Gujarat, Portuguese and Diu)
**Front:** What does the 2023 Prelims Q49 route test for Gujarat?
**Definition (learn):** A sequence question on Gujarat rulers, the Portuguese and the Diu surrender, a direct Topic-08 route.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: Gujarat rulers, Portuguese relations and Diu (✅ owner 08 §20.1).
- Answer chain: Mahmud Begarha resisted; Bahadur Shah conceded Bassein/Diu-related rights under pressure (✅ owner 08 §5).
- Official key unavailable locally; answer not recorded (✅ owner 08 §20.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Prelims GS-I Q49 (✅ owner 08 PYQ block).
**Links:** MED-2010, MED-2011, MED-2012.
**Trap:** Do not attribute the Diu surrender to Mahmud Begarha (✅ owner 08 §20.2).
**Mains hook:** Gujarat's port and Portuguese pressure answer.
**Back:** *"Forty-nine: Begarha resists, Bahadur bends at Diu."*

### Flashcard MED-2029 — PYQ route: 2023 Prelims Q45 (adjacent) and 2026 Prelims Q37 (Ahom)
**Front:** Which adjacent PYQs sit near the five-state core, and how should they be used?
**Definition (learn):** Two adjacent routes: 2023 Q45 (Hoysala, Gahadavala, Kakatiya, Yadava founding period) and 2026 Q37 (Ahom Moidams). Both lie outside the five-state core.
**Breakdown:**
- 2023 Prelims Q45: direct route but adjacent to the five-state core (✅ owner 08 §20.1).
- 2026 Prelims Q37: adjacent Art & Culture route; provisional key only (✅ owner 08 §20.1).
- Answer: keep the political-administrative context here; leave architecture and UNESCO to Art & Culture.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Q45; 2026 Q37 (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** MED-2025 (Moidams), MED-2024 (Ahom).
**Trap:** Do not claim the Hoysala/Yadava founding period is a five-state route. Do not treat the provisional 2026 key as confirmed.
**Mains hook:** Not a Topic-08 Mains route.
**Back:** *"Forty-five is south-west; thirty-seven is Ahom's mound."*

### Flashcard MED-2030 — Mains thesis for regional kingdoms (10/15/20 marks)
**Front:** How should a regional kingdoms answer be built at 10, 15 and 20 marks?
**Definition (learn):** The executable answer scaffolds from the owner's Mains architecture.
**Breakdown:**
- 10 marks: Bengal + Gujarat examples; conclusion: creative, not residual (✅ owner 08 §8.4).
- 15 marks: add Kashmir tolerance, Jaunpur learning, balance of power (✅ owner 08 §8.4).
- 20 marks: five-state deployment, Malwa geopolitics, Ahom variant, graded verdict (✅ owner 08 §8.4).
- Thesis: "regional states were autonomous polities, not symptoms of decline" (✅ owner 08 §8.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Word limits: 10 marks / 150 words; 15 and 20 marks / 250 words (✅ owner 08 §21).
**Links:** MED-2001, MED-2022, MED-2023, MED-2026.
**Trap:** Avoid a three-state essay; the 20-mark answer must cover all five states plus the source limit.
**Mains hook:** The question is political pluralism, not regional decline.
**Back:** *"Ten: two states. Fifteen: five states. Twenty: five plus a source limit."*

### Flashcard MED-2031 — Bengal's trade: ports, textiles, China and re-export
**Front:** What sustained Bengal's economy beyond agriculture?
**Definition (learn):** Bengal's prosperity from ports, textiles and maritime exchange, including re-export of Chinese goods.
**Breakdown:**
- Chittagong and Satgaon routes linked Bengal textiles, rice, silk and maritime trade (✅ owner 08 §11.2; adv. 08 §4 on Chittagong).
- Bengal exported fine textiles and re-exported Chinese goods (✅ adv. 08 §4).
- Ghiyasuddin Azam Shah's China contacts reflect the same trade orbit (✅ owner 08 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Chittagong; Satgaon; textiles; rice; silk (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** MED-2004, MED-2006, MED-2033.
**Trap:** "Bengal's prosperity was only agrarian" is wrong (✅ adv. 08 §5).
**Mains hook:** Bengal's wealth required state capacity and routes, not just rivers.
**Back:** *"Chittagong carries cloth and China: Bengal sells, Bengal re-sells."*

### Flashcard MED-2032 — Advanced concept: regionalisation as political economy and culture as statecraft
**Front:** What is the advanced theme of the regional kingdoms unit?
**Definition (learn):** Ports, routes, agrarian hinterlands and cultural capital made regional states durable; rulers used architecture, literature and patronage to legitimise power (✅ adv. 08 §1).
**Breakdown:**
- Regionalisation was not political collapse alone (✅ adv. 08 §1).
- Gujarat and Malwa were coveted because they linked fertile interiors, routes and overseas trade (✅ adv. 08 §1).
- Culture as statecraft: Bengal's brick architecture, Gujarat's Jain-derived style, Jaunpur's arches, Kashmir's reconciliation (✅ adv. 08 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Advanced owner (✅ adv. 08).
**Links:** MED-2001, MED-2023, MED-2033.
**Trap:** Do not present advanced theme as a required core answer; treat it as enrichment.
**Mains hook:** Evaluate architecture and language patronage as tools of regional legitimacy (⚠️ owner Mains angle).
**Back:** *"Ports pay, patrons praise: regional power is political economy plus culture."*

### Flashcard MED-2033 — Advanced: Kosi as a Bengal boundary after Firuz Tughlaq
**Front:** What boundary did Firuz Tughlaq's Bengal campaign fix, and what is its advanced significance?
**Definition (learn):** The Kosi river was fixed as a boundary after Firuz Tughlaq's Bengal campaign; Ilyas remained independent.
**Breakdown:**
- Kosi fixed as boundary after Firuz Tughlaq's Bengal campaign (✅ adv. 08 §4).
- Ilyas remained independent (✅ adv. 08 §4).
- The boundary shows Delhi's limit, not Bengal's subordination.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kosi boundary; Firuz Tughlaq's campaign (✅ adv. 08 §4; date not given in owner — ⚠️).
**Links:** MED-2002, MED-2006, MED-2026.
**Trap:** "Kosi boundary = Bengal subordinate" is wrong; boundary = mutual limit.
**Mains hook:** Use the Kosi to illustrate that frontier lines in medieval India often followed rivers and logistics.
**Back:** *"Kosi marks the line: Delhi stops, Bengal stands."*

### Flashcard MED-2034 — Advanced: Kashmir's Rishi-Sufi milieu and Zainul Abidin's offices
**Front:** What is the advanced Kashmir point on religious and cultural life?
**Definition (learn):** Kashmir's Rishi and Sufi traditions interacting with Persianate court culture and Sanskrit learning.
**Breakdown:**
- Persianate court culture interacted with Sanskrit learning, Kashmiri language, Sufi and Rishi traditions (✅ owner 08 §15.2).
- Zainul Abidin: restoration of temples, libraries and land grants; abolition of jizyah; high offices for Hindus (✅ adv. 08 §1).
- Valley ecology, agriculture and shawl/craft traditions shaped state capacity (✅ owner 08 §15.2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rishi-Sufi interaction (✅ owner 08 §15.2; adv. 08 §1).
**Links:** MED-2019, MED-2020, MED-2018.
**Trap:** Do not present Kashmir's Islamisation as uniform and peaceful (✅ adv. 08 §5).
**Mains hook:** Zainul Abidin's policy as political reconciliation after sectarian persecution (✅ adv. 08 §1).
**Back:** *"Rishi and Sufi share the valley; Zainul restores the shrines."*

### Flashcard MED-2035 — Advanced: Gujarat and Malwa in Deccan affairs
**Front:** Why were Gujarat and Malwa not isolated from Deccan politics?
**Definition (learn):** Gujarat and Malwa were drawn into Deccan affairs over Berar, the Konkan coast and trade routes.
**Breakdown:**
- Berar, Konkan and trade routes linked the western sultanates to the Deccan (✅ adv. 08 §5).
- Mahmud Gawan's Gujarat cooperation against Malwa shows shifting coalitions (⚠️ this is a Vijayanagara–Bahmani card cross-ref; owner 09 §14.2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No new dates in owner for this point.
**Links:** MED-2022, MED-2036.
**Trap:** Do not claim Gujarat and Malwa were isolated; this is a common examiner trap (✅ adv. 08 §5).
**Mains hook:** Regional states interacted across the Deccan, showing regional rather than communal alignments.
**Back:** *"Berar and Konkan pull Gujarat and Malwa into the Deccan."*

### Flashcard MED-2036 — Advanced: Bengal's inclusion without equality (Husain Shah)
**Front:** How should Bengal's inclusion of Hindu officials be stated in an answer?
**Definition (learn):** A bounded claim: court inclusion of Hindu officials is evidence of patronage, not evidence of social equality or absence of conflict.
**Breakdown:**
- Husain Shah offered high offices to Hindus including Rupa and Sanatan (✅ owner 08 §1).
- Court inclusion ≠ universal social equality (✅ owner 08 §11.2).
- Advanced: "Bengal's prosperity was only agrarian" is a trap (✅ adv. 08 §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Husain Shah 1494–1519 (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** MED-2005, MED-2031.
**Trap:** Do not summarise as "Bengal was tolerant"; say "court inclusion under Husain Shah".
**Mains hook:** Composite culture as evidenced interaction, not uncomplicated harmony (✅ owner 08 §8.2).
**Back:** *"Court includes, society still divides: say inclusion, not equality."*

### Flashcard MED-2037 — Advanced: source criticism for Ahom Buranjis and paik
**Front:** What source-criticism and institutional caution applies to Ahom material?
**Definition (learn):** The Buranjis are indispensable court chronicles but not neutral; the paik is a service obligation, not chattel slavery.
**Breakdown:**
- Buranjis: court chronicles; indispensable for sequence and institutions, not neutral (✅ adv. 08 §2, ⚠️ advanced framing).
- Paik: rotational service linked households, labour and military mobilisation; describe obligation precisely (✅ adv. 08 §2).
- Owner: "The Buranjis are indispensable court chronicles but not neutral or all contemporaneous in surviving form" (✅ owner 08 §16.2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Buranjis; paik (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** MED-2024, MED-2025.
**Trap:** "Forced labour" is not an exact description for paik; "chattel slavery" is wrong (✅ owner 08 §16.1).
**Mains hook:** Source criticism is a Mains marker: evidence claim, date, genre, limit.
**Back:** *"Buranji tells, paik serves, neither is a census."*

### GROUP — 09 Vijayanagara & the Bahmani Kingdom (Deccan)

### Flashcard MED-2038 — Vijayanagara and Bahmani as recombination, not an empty space
**Front:** Why are Vijayanagara and the Bahmani kingdom understood as regional recombination rather than creation from nothing?
**Definition (learn):** Two Deccan powers formed from the wreckage of Delhi's southern control, Hoysala, Madurai, Warangal/Velama and Reddi powers, Karnataka nayakas and coastal trade (✅ owner 09 §11).
**Breakdown:**
- Delhi's unstable southern control interacted with surviving Hoysalas, the Madurai Sultanate, Warangal/Velama and Reddi powers (✅ owner 09 §11).
- Karnataka nayakas, Tungabhadra–Krishna routes and coastal trade supplied the material base (✅ owner 09 §11).
- Contested zones: Tungabhadra doab, Krishna-Godavari basin, Konkan/Goa coast (✅ owner 09 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** The two kingdoms dominated south of the Vindhyas for more than two centuries (✅ owner 09 §1).
**Links:** MED-2039 (doab), MED-2040 (Vijayanagara origin), MED-2041 (Bahmani origin), MED-2066 (horses).
**Trap:** Do not present the conflict as a clash of "Hindu" and "Muslim" civilisations; the owner's evidence shows geography, revenue, forts, horses and ports (✅ owner 09 §5, §14).
**Mains hook:** "The Deccan was integrated by conflict as much as by culture" — argue that recombination and conflict created a shared political economy.
**Back:** *"Two powers, three zones: doab, delta and coast, not a civilisational line."*

### Flashcard MED-2039 — Tungabhadra doab and the Raichur–Mudgal forts
**Front:** Why was the Tungabhadra (Raichur) doab the central prize between Vijayanagara and the Bahmanis?
**Definition (learn):** The fertile doab between the Tungabhadra and Krishna rivers, controlled through forts, cultivation and frontier revenue.
**Breakdown:**
- Fertile and strategically placed between the two powers (✅ owner 09 §1).
- Holding a doab needed defended routes, garrisons, supplies and local cooperation (✅ owner 09 §14.1).
- Raichur and Mudgal forts linked frontier to supply (✅ owner 09 §19).
- Deva Raya I's Tungabhadra dam is the agrarian anchor of the zone (see MED-2043).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mudkal/Tungabhadra war of 1367 (✅ owner 09 §1).
**Links:** MED-2038, MED-2042, MED-2043.
**Trap:** Battle victory alone did not hold a doab; forts, supply and cooperation did (✅ owner 09 §14.1).
**Mains hook:** Use the doab to explain why Deccan warfare was resource warfare as much as dynastic rivalry.
**Back:** *"Doab feeds, forts hold, Raichur is the lock."*

### Flashcard MED-2040 — Vijayanagara's foundation: c. 1336, Harihara and Bukka
**Front:** What is the conventional foundation date of Vijayanagara and who were its founders?
**Definition (learn):** Harihara and Bukka, the brothers whose coronation c. 1336 is the conventional foundation anchor of Vijayanagara.
**Breakdown:**
- c. 1336: conventional foundation/coronation anchor (✅ owner 09 §11).
- Traditions connect the brothers to Warangal/Kampili, capture and conversion, reconversion and Vidyaranya (✅ owner 09 §11).
- Origins and Vidyaranya's founding role are debated; inscriptions and urban growth give firmer evidence (✅ owner 09 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 1336 (✅ owner 09 mini-timeline). Harihara I, Bukka I (✅ owner 09 §12).
**Links:** MED-2038, MED-2056 (ladder), MED-2041.
**Trap:** Do not state "Vijayanagara was founded in 1336 as a Hindu bulwark"; the owner says the "Hindu bulwark" framing is a later overlay (⚠️ owner §8.3).
**Mains hook:** Foundation narratives are later constructions; evaluate them against inscriptions and urban evidence.
**Back:** *"Thirteen thirty-six, Harihara and Bukka: a tradition, not a single birth."*

### Flashcard MED-2041 — Bahmani's independence: 1347 and Hasan Gangu
**Front:** When and by whom was the Bahmani kingdom founded?
**Definition (learn):** The Bahmani kingdom, founded in 1347 by Alauddin Hasan Bahman Shah (Hasan Gangu in later tradition), with Gulbarga as the early capital.
**Breakdown:**
- Independent rule began 1347; Wikipedia gives 3 August 1347 and says Zafar Khan, also called Hasan Gangu, was crowned Ala-ud-Din Bahman Shah (✅ wiki: Bahmani Sultanate; owner 09 §11).
- "Hasan Gangu" and service under a Brahman named Gangu come largely from later tradition, especially Firishta (✅ owner 09 §11).
- Claimed descent and title traditions enhanced legitimacy but do not establish biological origin (✅ owner 09 §11).
- Early capital: Gulbarga; later shift to Bidar under Ahmad Shah I (MED-2046).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1347 (✅ owner 09). Gulbarga early capital (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2040, MED-2046, MED-2050.
**Trap:** Do not give "Hasan Gangu" as a verified personal name without the Firishta caveat (✅ owner 09 §11).
**Mains hook:** Use the Firishta tradition as a case of how later chroniclers shape origin stories.
**Back:** *"Forty-seven, Bahman founds, Gangu is legend: Gulbarga is the seat."*

### Flashcard MED-2042 — The 1367 Mudkal war and the treaty idea
**Front:** What was special about the 1367 Vijayanagara–Bahmani war?
**Definition (learn):** The 1367 war over the Mudkal/Tungabhadra doab, noted for artillery on both sides and for an agreement to avoid slaughter of helpless civilians.
**Breakdown:**
- Artillery noted on both sides (✅ owner 09 mini-timeline; adv. 09 §4: "first noted use of artillery").
- Agreement to avoid slaughter of helpless and unarmed inhabitants in future wars (✅ adv. 09 §1).
- Mudkal/Tungabhadra location (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1367 (✅ owner 09 mini-timeline). The "first noted use of artillery" wording is the advanced owner's reading (✅ adv. 09 §4).
**Links:** MED-2039, MED-2038, MED-2066.
**Trap:** Do not say artillery was introduced by Vijayanagara; the owner says both sides used it. "First noted" is a reporting claim, not a proof of first use (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** The 1367 agreement shows war norms in medieval Deccan, complicating a "pure violence" narrative.
**Back:** *"Sixty-seven: guns on both sides, mercy for the helpless, in theory."*

### Flashcard MED-2043 — Deva Raya I (1404–22): dam, canal and Firuz Shah marriage
**Front:** What did Deva Raya I achieve between 1404 and 1422?
**Definition (learn):** A Sangama ruler who built a dam across the Tungabhadra, dug canals to raise revenue, and made a political marriage with Firuz Shah Bahmani.
**Breakdown:**
- Dam across the Tungabhadra; canals increased revenue (✅ owner 09 §4).
- Political marriage with Firuz Shah Bahmani (✅ owner 09 §14.2; adv. 09 mini-timeline).
- Conflict with Firuz Shah Bahmani (✅ owner 09 mini-timeline).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1404–22 (✅ owner 09). Tungabhadra dam and canals (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2044 (Deva Raya II), MED-2039, MED-2071 (PYQ 2023 Q48), MED-2045.
**Trap:** Deva Raya I = dam and canal; Deva Raya II = military adaptation (✅ owner 09 §25). Do not swap them.
**Mains hook:** Water works linked the doab to revenue and state capacity; a marriage alliance did not end conflict.
**Back:** *"Deva I dams the Tunga; Deva II drills the cavalry."*

### Flashcard MED-2044 — Deva Raya II (1425–46): military adaptation and Muslim cavalry
**Front:** How did Deva Raya II strengthen Vijayanagara's army?
**Definition (learn):** A Sangama ruler (1425–46) who reorganised the army by employing Muslim cavalrymen and archers and learning from Sultanate methods.
**Breakdown:**
- Muslim cavalrymen and archers employed (✅ owner 09 §1).
- Army reorganisation; peak prosperity (✅ owner 09 mini-timeline).
- Response to Bahmani superiority in horses and mounted archery (✅ adv. 09 §1).
- Travellers Nicolo Conti and Abdur Razzaq describe the empire under his reign (✅ owner 09 §1, §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1425–46 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2043, MED-2063 (travellers), MED-2066 (horses).
**Trap:** "Vijayanagara never used Muslim soldiers" is wrong (✅ owner 09 §5).
**Mains hook:** Military adaptation crossed religious boundaries and defeats rigid communal framing (✅ owner 09 §8.2 unit 2).
**Back:** *"Deva Two hires horsemen of both faiths: skill over sect."*

### Flashcard MED-2045 — Firuz Shah Bahmani (1397–1422): learning, ports and campaigns
**Front:** What is Firuz Shah Bahmani's exam-relevant profile?
**Definition (learn):** The Bahmani sultan (1397–1422) who encouraged sciences and languages, developed ports and conducted campaigns and alliances.
**Breakdown:**
- Learning: encouraged sciences and languages (✅ adv. 09 §4).
- Ports, campaigns, alliances and political marriage (✅ owner 09 §13).
- Conflict with Deva Raya I; political marriage (✅ owner 09 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1397–1422 (✅ owner 09 §13).
**Links:** MED-2043, MED-2046, MED-2047.
**Trap:** Firuz Shah (Bahmani) is not Firuz Shah Tughlaq of Delhi; keep the dynasty explicit.
**Mains hook:** Firuz's learning and port policy shows Bahmani statecraft beyond warfare.
**Back:** *"Firuz, Bahmani: learning, ports, marriages, and wars."*

### Flashcard MED-2046 — Ahmad Shah I Bahmani (1422–36): Warangal and the shift to Bidar
**Front:** What did Ahmad Shah I Bahmani do for the Bahmani kingdom?
**Definition (learn):** The Bahmani sultan (1422–36) who expanded toward Warangal and moved the capital from Gulbarga to Bidar.
**Breakdown:**
- Warangal expansion (✅ owner 09 §13).
- Association with Gesu Daraz's memory (✅ owner 09 §13, §22).
- Capital shift from Gulbarga to Bidar (✅ owner 09 §13; adv. 09 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1422–36 (✅ owner 09 §13). Capital shift to Bidar: Wikipedia credits Ahmad Shah I but gives 1425 in the infobox and 1429 in the body text (✅ wiki; conflict flagged).
**Links:** MED-2041, MED-2062 (Bidar), MED-2047.
**Trap:** Ahmad Shah I (Bahmani) is not Ahmad Shah I of Gujarat (MED-2009). Keep the state explicit.
**Mains hook:** The capital shift is recorded in the owner; the reason is not stated there, so do not assign one without a source (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Ahmad I shifts the seat: Gulbarga to Bidar."*

### Flashcard MED-2047 — Mahmud Gawan: minister, not sultan; Goa, Dabhol and execution
**Front:** Who was Mahmud Gawan and what did he achieve before his execution?
**Definition (learn):** The Bahmani wakil-i-sultanat (leading minister), c. 1463–1481/82, who expanded to Goa and Dabhol, reformed provinces and built the Bidar madrasa.
**Breakdown:**
- Minister (wakil-i-sultanat / prime minister), not a sultan (✅ owner 09 §13; adv. 09 §4).
- Captured or controlled Goa and Dabhol (✅ owner 09 §1, §4).
- Divided the kingdom into eight tarafs (✅ owner 09 §17; MED-2048).
- Built a three-storeyed madrasa at Bidar with coloured tiles (✅ adv. 09 §1).
- Executed in 1482 (adv. owner) or c. 1481–82 (basic owner). Wikipedia says he was executed in 1481 and the sultan regretted the order until his death in 1482 (✅ wiki: Bahmani Sultanate). Both owners and Wikipedia fall in the 1481–82 window; state it as such.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 1463–1481/82 (✅ owner 09 §13). Execution c. 1481/82 (✅ owner 09 §17; adv. 09 §1 reads 1482).
**Links:** MED-2048 (eight tarafs), MED-2049 (Deccani–Afaqi), MED-2062 (madrasa), MED-2050 (breakup).
**Trap:** Mahmud Gawan was not a sultan and not the founder of the Bahmani kingdom (✅ owner 09 §25). "He was only a conqueror" is also wrong; he was an administrator and patron (✅ adv. 09 §5).
**Mains hook:** Assess Mahmud Gawan as a centraliser whose reforms could not survive factional politics (✅ owner 09 §7 Mains angle).
**Back:** *"Gawan the minister: Goa and Dabhol gained, tarafs reshaped, madrasa built, life lost in faction."*

### Flashcard MED-2048 — Eight tarafs: Mahmud Gawan's provincial reform
**Front:** What did Mahmud Gawan's reform do to the Bahmani provinces?
**Definition (learn):** Gawan divided four larger tarafs into eight, fixed salaries and troop obligations, sought land measurement and stronger fort control.
**Breakdown:**
- Four larger tarafs subdivided into eight (✅ owner 09 §17).
- Fixed nobles' salaries and obligations (✅ adv. 09 §1).
- Reserved khalisa land more directly for the sultan (✅ adv. 09 §1; owner §17 table).
- Attempted land measurement; sought direct fort governor appointments (✅ owner 09 §17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Eight tarafs (✅ owner 09 §2, §4).
**Links:** MED-2047, MED-2060 (administration).
**Trap:** Taraf reform was not a completed uniform bureaucracy (✅ owner 09 §17). Do not call it a finished bureaucratic revolution.
**Mains hook:** Compare Gawan's reform with Vijayanagara's amaram (MED-2058) as two responses to fiscal-military pressure.
**Back:** *"Four become eight: taraf, khalisa, fort, salary — a reform in progress."*

### Flashcard MED-2049 — Deccani vs Afaqi factions
**Front:** What were the Deccani and Afaqi (gharib) factions in the Bahmani kingdom?
**Definition (learn):** Changing faction labels involving settlement, patronage, office, regional networks and sometimes sectarian alignments.
**Breakdown:**
- Deccanis (old-comers) vs Afaqis/gharibs (new-comers) (✅ adv. 09 §2).
- Not pure races, sealed ethnic blocs or permanent parties (✅ owner 09 §17).
- Factional strife led to Gawan's execution and accelerated disintegration (✅ adv. 09 §1; owner §17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gawan's execution c. 1481/82 (✅ owner 09 §17).
**Links:** MED-2047, MED-2050.
**Trap:** Bahmani disintegration was not caused by Vijayanagara alone; factionalism was crucial (✅ owner 09 §5). Gawan's execution "accelerated, but did not single-handedly cause" disintegration (✅ owner 09 §17).
**Mains hook:** Argue that factional politics, not only external war, drove Bahmani breakup.
**Back:** *"Deccani and Afaqi: old settlers vs new comers, not two races."*

### Flashcard MED-2050 — Bahmani breakup into five successor states
**Front:** Name the five successor states that emerged from the Bahmani kingdom.
**Definition (learn):** The successor states formed through a process of breakup, not a single declaration.
**Breakdown:**
- Ahmadnagar, Bijapur, Golconda, Berar and Bidar (✅ owner 09 §13).
- Adv. order: Golconda, Bijapur, Ahmadnagar, Berar and Bidar (✅ adv. 09 §4). Wikipedia lists Ahmednagar, Berar, Bidar, Bijapur and Golconda (✅ wiki: Bahmani Sultanate).
- Breakup was a process; late Bahmani court faction and provincial autonomy drove it (✅ owner 09 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five successor states (✅ owner 09; adv. 09).
**Links:** MED-2047, MED-2049, MED-2054 (Talikota coalition).
**Trap:** Berar should not be automatically inserted into every Talikota participant list (✅ owner 09 §15). Also Mahmud Gawan was not the founder of any successor state.
**Mains hook:** Bahmani breakup as a multi-causal process: succession, faction, provincial autonomy, external pressure (✅ owner 09 §8.3).
**Back:** *"Ahmad, Bijapur, Golconda, Berar, Bidar: five seats, one parent."*

### Flashcard MED-2051 — Krishnadevaraya (1509–29): restored order and patronage
**Front:** What defines Krishnadevaraya's reign?
**Definition (learn):** The Tuluva ruler (1509–29) who restored internal order, defeated rivals, patronised literature and built tanks and towns.
**Breakdown:**
- Restored internal order; defeated rivals (✅ owner 09 §1).
- Literature: Telugu *Amuktamalyada* combines devotion with normative reflections on kingship (✅ owner 09 §23).
- Irrigation and urban patronage (✅ owner 09 §12).
- Raichur campaigns (✅ owner 09 §12).
- Broad religious freedom noted by Barbosa (✅ adv. 09 §2).
- After his death, factional politics and Rama Raja's diplomacy led to 1565 (✅ owner 09 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1509–29 (✅ owner 09 §12). Basic timeline note: "OCR gives 1509–30" (⚠️ owner flag).
**Links:** MED-2052 (Bhatkal), MED-2053 (Nuniz), MED-2054 (Talikota), MED-2074 (Ashtadiggajas).
**Trap:** Krishnadevaraya = Tuluva; Achyuta Raya followed him (✅ owner 09 §25).
**Mains hook:** Krishnadevaraya's reign shows patronage and cooperative diplomacy as statecraft; the 1565 collapse shows the limits of that system.
**Back:** *"Krishna restores, writes, builds; after him, faction brings the fall."*

### Flashcard MED-2052 — The Bhatkal fort attribution (PYQ 2024 Q56)
**Front:** What do the 2024 Prelims Q56 and the Bhatkal fort attribution test?
**Definition (learn):** The medieval ruler who allowed the Portuguese a fort at Bhatkal; the attribution rests mainly on Portuguese narratives.
**Breakdown:**
- PYQ 2024 Prelims GS-I Q56: medieval ruler who allowed the Portuguese a fort at Bhatkal (✅ owner 09 PYQ block).
- Official 2024 Set-A key available locally, but the answer is not recorded in the owner (✅ owner 09 PYQ block).
- Attribution rests on Barbosa and Paes; safest point is cooperation with source caution (✅ owner 09 §4, §8.2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Prelims Q56 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2051, MED-2053, MED-2066.
**Trap:** Do not assert an unqualified sanad or charter claim (✅ owner 09 §8.2 unit 7). The owner specifically warns that the "exact wording varies" across sources.
**Mains hook:** Evaluate Vijayanagara's pragmatic Portuguese relations: horses, coastal trade and diplomacy.
**Back:** *"Bhatkal to the Portuguese: say cooperation, not a sworn charter."*

### Flashcard MED-2053 — Nuniz and women's specialised roles (PYQ 2021 Q35)
**Front:** Which Portuguese traveller does the 2021 Prelims Q35 associate with women's expertise in wrestling, astrology, accounting and soothsaying?
**Definition (learn):** The 2021 official paper attributes this list to Nuniz (Fernao Nuniz, c. 1530s).
**Breakdown:**
- Attribution: Nuniz (✅ owner 09 §4, §21 and PYQ block).
- Older repository note attached a similar list to Paes; use "Portuguese accounts" unless authorship is checked (✅ owner 09 §1).
- Official key for 2021 Q35 unavailable locally (✅ owner 09 PYQ block).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Nuniz c. 1530s; Paes c. 1520s (✅ owner 09 §10).
**Links:** MED-2063 (travellers), MED-2052, MED-2065.
**Trap:** Do not shift authorship to Paes; the owner says "do not shift authorship to Paes" (✅ owner 09 §21).
**Mains hook:** Women's visible specialised roles in elite and urban life, not a census of all women (✅ owner 09 §21).
**Back:** *"Nuniz lists women's skills: wrestle, read stars, keep accounts, foretell."*

### Flashcard MED-2054 — Talikota (1565): causal chain and coalition
**Front:** What caused the 1565 Battle of Talikota (Rakshasa-Tangadi / Bannihatti) and what was the result?
**Definition (learn):** The 1565 battle in which a Deccan sultanate coalition defeated Vijayanagara, with Rama Raya captured and executed and the metropolitan core sacked.
**Breakdown:**
- Causal chain: Rama Raya's repeated intervention in Deccan rivalries; diplomatic humiliation; frontier contests; temporary coalition (✅ owner 09 §15).
- Participants: commonly Ahmadnagar, Bijapur, Golconda and Bidar; Berar not automatic (✅ owner 09 §15).
- Rama Raya captured and executed; Hampi's core sacked (✅ owner 09 §15; adv. 09 mini-timeline).
- Cavalry, artillery/firearms, commanders and battlefield contingency interacted (✅ owner 09 §15).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1565 (✅ owner 09 mini-timeline). Avoid exact troop or casualty figures (✅ owner 09 §15).
**Links:** MED-2050, MED-2055 (continuity), MED-2057 (Aliya Rama Raya), MED-2067 (trap).
**Trap:** Talikota was not "one sudden battle"; it followed balance-of-power diplomacy and coalition (✅ adv. 09 §5). Do not use it as an instant end of the empire (MED-2067).
**Mains hook:** Talikota as a turning point, not a simple end date; coalition warfare overrode individual strength (✅ owner 09 §8.2 unit 6).
**Back:** *"Sixty-five at Talikota: Rama Raya falls, Hampi sacked, empire lingers."*

### Flashcard MED-2055 — After Talikota: Aravidu continuity and nayaka autonomy
**Front:** What survived after 1565?
**Definition (learn):** The post-Talikota transition: Aravidu rulers continued from new centres with reduced reach, and nayaka powers grew more autonomous.
**Breakdown:**
- Tirumala and the Aravidu line continued from Penukonda and later centres (✅ owner 09 §15).
- Nayaka powers in Madurai, Tanjavur, Gingee and elsewhere became more autonomous (✅ owner 09 §15).
- Political, revenue, temple and commercial networks survived in altered forms (✅ owner 09 §15).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tirumala (Aravidu) (✅ owner 09 §12).
**Links:** MED-2054, MED-2056, MED-2058.
**Trap:** "Talikota immediately ended every remnant" is wrong (✅ owner 09 §5). Nayaka systems cannot be projected back unchanged (✅ owner 09 §8.2 unit 4).
**Mains hook:** Continuity and decline in one answer: Talikota ended great-power status, not the polity in one instant.
**Back:** *"Sixty-five cracks the crown; Aravidu moves the court and nayakas gain."*

### Flashcard MED-2056 — Vijayanagara dynastic ladder
**Front:** What is the exam-safe order of Vijayanagara dynasties and key rulers?
**Definition (learn):** The line Sangama → Saluva → Tuluva → Aravidu with its key rulers.
**Breakdown:**
- Sangama: Harihara I, Bukka I, Deva Raya I (1404–22), Deva Raya II (1425–46); late Sangama crisis with Mallikarjuna/Virupaksha phases (✅ owner 09 §12).
- Saluva: Saluva Narasimha, who restored order through dynastic seizure (✅ owner 09 §12).
- Tuluva: Vira Narasimha; Krishnadevaraya (1509–29); Achyuta Raya (1529–42) (✅ owner 09 §12).
- Nominal Tuluva/oligarchic phase: Sadashiva Raya and Aliya Rama Raya (✅ owner 09 §12).
- Aravidu: Tirumala and successors (✅ owner 09 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Achyuta Raya 1529–42 (✅ owner 09 §12).
**Links:** MED-2040, MED-2043, MED-2044, MED-2051, MED-2055, MED-2057.
**Trap:** "Sangama → Saluva → Tuluva → Aravidu" is a repository trap ladder (✅ owner 09 §25). Krishnadevaraya is Tuluva; Achyuta follows him.
**Mains hook:** Dynastic replacement did not erase the polity, but no single administrative model operated unchanged (✅ owner 09 §12).
**Back:** *"Sangama, Saluva, Tuluva, Aravidu: four houses, one city."*

### Flashcard MED-2057 — Aliya Rama Raya: effective power, not Aravidu founder
**Front:** What was Aliya Rama Raya's role before Talikota?
**Definition (learn):** The nominal-Tuluva-phase power-holder who exercised effective power before 1565 and shaped shifting Deccan alliances.
**Breakdown:**
- Effective power before Talikota (✅ owner 09 §25).
- Balance-of-power diplomacy among Deccan sultanates (✅ owner 09 §14.2).
- Not the founder of the Aravidu dynasty (✅ owner 09 §25).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pre-1565 (✅ owner 09 §12).
**Links:** MED-2054, MED-2056.
**Trap:** Aliya Rama Raya ≠ Aravidu founder (✅ owner 09 §25).
**Mains hook:** Rama Raya's interventions made him a catalyst for coalition warfare.
**Back:** *"Rama Raya rules, Aravidu follows: not the same founder."*

### Flashcard MED-2058 — Amaram and nayaka service vs the Sultanate iqta
**Front:** How did amaram-nayaka service differ from the transferable Sultanate iqta?
**Definition (learn):** Amaram linked territorial revenue to nayaka military service; many nayakas built hereditary local bases, but the system was not an iqta.
**Breakdown:**
- Amaram: revenue-service relationship supporting troops and local administration (✅ owner 09 §16).
- Nayakas: military-intermediary powers; many acquired hereditary bases but remained politically negotiated (✅ owner 09 §16).
- Iqta (Sultanate): transferable assignments; advanced owner states hereditary nayaks could not be transferred like Sultanate muqtis (✅ adv. 09 §1).
- Practice varied by dynasty and region (✅ owner 09 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No dates; generalised system (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2059, MED-2055, MED-2060.
**Trap:** Amaram is not the same as iqta; "nayaka" is not a Sultanate office (✅ owner 09 §1 and adv. 09 §1).
**Mains hook:** Fiscal-military mobilisation: nayakas vs muqtis, showing different service relationships.
**Back:** *"Amaram stays hereditary; iqta moves on."*

### Flashcard MED-2059 — Vijayanagara administration: four competing models
**Front:** What are the four interpretations of Vijayanagara's state?
**Definition (learn):** Scholarly models of the state: segmentary, centralised, military-fiscal and integrative/negotiated.
**Breakdown:**
- Segmentary: ritual sovereignty and strong local units limit central control (✅ owner 09 §16).
- Centralised: taxation, standing forces, court, records, capital show substantial royal capacity (✅ owner 09 §16).
- Military-fiscal: revenue assignments, nayakas, forts and cavalry tie territory to war (✅ owner 09 §16).
- Integrative/negotiated: core control and layered centre-periphery bargains changing by dynasty and region (✅ owner 09 §16).
- Safest verdict: variable, negotiated centralisation (✅ owner 09 §16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Institutions: Raya; ministerial council; rajya/mandala; nadu, sthala, grama; amaram; nayaka; ayagar (✅ owner 09 §16).
**Links:** MED-2058, MED-2060.
**Trap:** Not "European feudalism", "timeless segmentary state" or "Delhi-style bureaucracy" (✅ owner 09 §16). Adv. 09 §5: "Vijayanagara was centralised despite limitations", not purely decentralised.
**Mains hook:** Compare models with evidence and limits, then give a graded verdict.
**Back:** *"Four lenses, one verdict: variable, negotiated centralisation."*

### Flashcard MED-2060 — Bahmani administration: wakil, taraf, khalisa, forts
**Front:** What institutions and office-holders structured Bahmani administration?
**Definition (learn):** The sultan and court, the wakil-i-sultanat, tarafs and tarafdars, the khalisa, fort governors and local revenue personnel.
**Breakdown:**
- Sultan and court: sovereignty, appointments, military and cultural patronage (✅ owner 09 §17).
- Wakil-i-sultanat / leading minister: high executive coordination; Mahmud Gawan the key case (✅ owner 09 §17).
- Taraf/tarafdar: provincial territorial and military-revenue power (✅ owner 09 §17).
- Khalisa: resources reserved more directly for the crown (✅ owner 09 §17).
- Forts and governors: frontier and provincial control (✅ owner 09 §17).
- Local revenue personnel: Deccani Brahmans and locally embedded groups linked court and countryside (✅ owner 09 §17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Eight tarafs under Gawan (✅ owner 09 §17).
**Links:** MED-2047, MED-2048, MED-2049, MED-2059.
**Trap:** Mahmud Gawan was minister, not a Bahmani sultan (✅ owner 09 §13).
**Mains hook:** Bahmani reform aimed at centralisation but was incomplete; compare with Vijayanagara's model (MED-2059).
**Back:** *"Wakil runs, taraf divides, khalisa keeps the crown's share."*

### Flashcard MED-2061 — Hampi (Vijayanagara) monuments and urban landscape
**Front:** Which Hampi monuments and labels must be handled with care?
**Definition (learn):** The coordinated fortified, sacred, royal, hydraulic, market and residential zones of Vijayanagara's capital.
**Breakdown:**
- Virupaksha temple: older sacred site expanded under Vijayanagara, not wholly built by one emperor (✅ owner 09 §24).
- Vitthala complex: ritual/architectural complex built in phases (✅ owner 09 §24).
- Hazara Rama temple: royal-centre monument and narrative relief evidence (✅ owner 09 §24).
- Raya gopuras: conspicuous patronage (✅ owner 09 §24).
- "Lotus Mahal" and "Elephant Stables": conventional labels; original name/function not securely established (✅ owner 09 §24).
- Abdur Razzaq praised seven enclosures, bazaars, canals and wealth (✅ adv. 09 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Seven enclosures (✅ adv. 09 §4).
**Links:** MED-2063 (Abdur Razzaq), MED-2062, MED-2065.
**Trap:** Do not state "Lotus Mahal" as a verified function; it is a conventional modern label (✅ owner 09 §24).
**Mains hook:** Urban landscapes show state, ritual and market integration, not only military power.
**Back:** *"Virupaksha predates, Vitthala builds in phases, Lotus is a label."*

### Flashcard MED-2062 — Bidar, Gulbarga and Mahmud Gawan's madrasa
**Front:** Which Bahmani monuments belong to Gulbarga, Bidar and Mahmud Gawan?
**Definition (learn):** The Gulbarga fort and Jama Masjid (early capital), the Bidar fort and urban court (later centre) and Mahmud Gawan's madrasa.
**Breakdown:**
- Gulbarga fort and Jama Masjid: early capital and military-religious landscape (✅ owner 09 §24).
- Bidar fort and urban court: later political-cultural centre (✅ owner 09 §24).
- Mahmud Gawan's madrasa at Bidar: three-storeyed, coloured tiles, facilities for scholars (✅ adv. 09 §1; owner §24).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gulbarga early; Bidar later (✅ owner 09 §13).
**Links:** MED-2041, MED-2046, MED-2047.
**Trap:** Gulbarga → Bidar sequence (✅ owner 09 §25). Mahmud Gawan's madrasa ≠ a Gulbarga building.
**Mains hook:** Gawan's madrasa shows learning patronage and transregional tile craft.
**Back:** *"Gulbarga first, Bidar later, Gawan's madrasa with coloured tiles."*

### Flashcard MED-2063 — Traveller sources: Conti, Abdur Razzaq, Paes, Barbosa, Nuniz
**Front:** Which foreign travellers describe Vijayanagara, and what date and bias should be noted?
**Definition (learn):** The travellers whose accounts give independent corroboration of Vijayanagara's scale, wealth and urban life.
**Breakdown:**
- Nicolo Conti, c. 1420s — Italian view of scale and city; hearsay needs cross-check (✅ owner 09 §10).
- Abdur Razzaq, 1440s — Timurid envoy under Deva Raya II; spectacular numbers are not a census (✅ owner 09 §10).
- Domingo Paes, c. 1520s — Portuguese observation of Krishnadevaraya's capital; selective visitor perspective (✅ owner 09 §10).
- Duarte Barbosa — noted broad freedom under Krishnadevaraya's religious policy (✅ adv. 09 §2).
- Fernao Nuniz, c. 1530s — Portuguese narrative; the 2021 PYQ route (✅ owner 09 §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Conti c. 1420s; Razzaq 1440s; Paes c. 1520s; Nuniz c. 1530s (✅ owner 09 §10).
**Links:** MED-2053 (Nuniz), MED-2052 (Bhatkal), MED-2061.
**Trap:** Fifteenth century = Conti and Razzaq; sixteenth = Paes and Nuniz (✅ owner 09 §25).
**Mains hook:** Evaluate travellers for economy, urbanism and political culture, with their biases stated (✅ owner 09 §7).
**Back:** *"Conti and Razzaq in the fifteenth, Paes and Nuniz in the sixteenth."*

### Flashcard MED-2064 — Court literature: Madhuravijayam and Amuktamalyada
**Front:** What are the two court texts that test the political culture of Vijayanagara?
**Definition (learn):** Gangadevi's Sanskrit *Madhuravijayam* (Madurai campaign) and Krishnadevaraya's Telugu *Amuktamalyada* (kingship and political counsel).
**Breakdown:**
- *Madhuravijayam*: links literary authorship, conquest memory and royal legitimacy (✅ owner 09 §23).
- *Amuktamalyada*: literary devotion with normative reflections on kingship; not an administrative gazette (✅ owner 09 §23).
- Both are literary-political evidence, not neutral reportage (✅ owner 09 §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gangadevi (Sanskrit); Krishnadevaraya (Telugu) (✅ owner 09 §10, §23).
**Links:** MED-2051, MED-2063, MED-2074.
**Trap:** Amuktamalyada is not an administrative gazette; it does not prove uniform administration (✅ owner 09 §10).
**Mains hook:** Literature as statecraft; use these texts to show ideals, not uniform practice.
**Back:** *"Gangadevi wins Madurai in verse; Krishna writes kingship in Telugu."*

### Flashcard MED-2065 — Vijayanagara coinage: varaha / pagoda
**Front:** What do Vijayanagara's gold coins illustrate?
**Definition (learn):** Gold varaha/pagoda issues that show titulature, metal and sovereignty claims.
**Breakdown:**
- Gold varaha/pagoda issues (✅ owner 09 §20).
- Coins illuminate sovereignty and monetisation; circulation does not map exact borders (✅ owner 09 §10, §20).
- Coin finds do not prove total cash penetration (✅ owner 09 §20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gold varaha; pagoda (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2021 (fanam), MED-2060, MED-2066.
**Trap:** Coin circulation ≠ political border; varaha ≠ fanam (fanam is a small gold coin of south-coastal trade, MED-2021).
**Mains hook:** Monetisation evidence needs caution; coins show claims, not total economic reach.
**Back:** *"Varaha and pagoda show the crown's gold, not the whole economy."*

### Flashcard MED-2066 — Horses, Goa and Dabhol: the strategic premium
**Front:** Why did horses and western ports matter to the Deccan powers?
**Definition (learn):** Imported horses from Arabia and Iran (via west-coast ports) created a strategic premium for cavalry.
**Breakdown:**
- Horses from Iran and Iraq essential to southern armies (✅ owner 09 §1).
- Goa and Dabhol connected maritime merchants to inland armies (✅ owner 09 §19).
- Horse trade did not alone decide victory (✅ owner 09 §19).
- Advanced: ports were strategic assets, not merely commercial centres (✅ adv. 09 concept).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Goa/Konkan; Dabhol (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2038, MED-2047, MED-2052, MED-2067.
**Trap:** "Goa mattered only after the Portuguese" is wrong; it was vital for horse imports (✅ owner 09 §5).
**Mains hook:** Military power depended on maritime trade and cavalry supply, not religion.
**Back:** *"Horses from the sea make cavalry; Goa was the gate before the Portuguese."*

### Flashcard MED-2067 — Trap: Talikota was not an instant end, and Vijayanagara not purely decentralised
**Front:** What are the two Talikota and Vijayanagara traps to avoid?
**Definition (learn):** Two linked errors: "1565 ended every remnant," and "Vijayanagara was a purely decentralised nayak state."
**Breakdown:**
- Talikota ended the great age; the kingdom lingered with shrinking territory (✅ owner 09 §5).
- Vijayanagara was not a purely decentralised nayak state; the advanced owner calls it a centralised state despite limitations (✅ adv. 09 §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1565 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2054, MED-2055, MED-2059.
**Trap:** The corrected answer: "variable, negotiated centralisation with a 1565 turning point".
**Mains hook:** Use the two errors to frame a graded verdict.
**Back:** *"Sixty-five ends the great age, not the polity; neither nayak-only nor pure centre."*

### Flashcard MED-2068 — Trap: Bahmani decline was not only Vijayanagara pressure
**Front:** Why is "Bahmani decline due to Vijayanagara" wrong?
**Definition (learn):** Bahmani decline was multi-causal: internal Deccani–Afaqi factionalism, provincial autonomy and external pressure.
**Breakdown:**
- Internal factionalism was crucial (✅ owner 09 §5).
- Gawan's execution accelerated the breakup but did not alone cause it (✅ owner 09 §17).
- Successor-state formation was a process (✅ owner 09 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 1481/82 (✅ owner 09 §17).
**Links:** MED-2047, MED-2049, MED-2050.
**Trap:** The examiner trap is a single-cause answer.
**Mains hook:** Multi-causal breakup argument.
**Back:** *"Factions first, Vijayanagara second: a multi-cause breakup."*

### Flashcard MED-2069 — Mains thesis: Vijayanagara and Bahmani (10/15/20 marks)
**Front:** How should a Deccan answer be built at 10, 15 and 20 marks?
**Definition (learn):** Executable scaffolds from the owner's Mains architecture.
**Breakdown:**
- 10 marks: geography thesis (doab/coast/horses) + Deva Raya II adaptation + Talikota qualification; conclude strategic, not communal (✅ owner 09 §8.4).
- 15 marks: add Mahmud Gawan, amaram comparison and traveller evidence (✅ owner 09 §8.4).
- 20 marks: full comparison across origins, capitals, nayaka/taraf, revenue, society, Talikota and evidence limits (✅ owner 09 §26).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 marks/150 words; 15 and 20 marks/250 words (✅ owner 09 §26).
**Links:** MED-2038, MED-2059, MED-2060, MED-2054.
**Trap:** Avoid communal and feudal labels; reject the "instant end" model.
**Mains hook:** The Deccan integrated by conflict as much as culture.
**Back:** *"Ten: geography. Fifteen: Gawan and travellers. Twenty: everything, graded."*

### Flashcard MED-2070 — PYQ route: 2021 Prelims Q35 (Nuniz and women)
**Front:** What does 2021 Prelims Q35 test?
**Definition (learn):** Women's expertise in Vijayanagara as described in the Portuguese account (Nuniz attribution in the paper).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: women's expertise in Vijayanagara in the Portuguese account (✅ owner 09 PYQ block).
- Official key unavailable locally; answer not inferred (✅ owner 09 PYQ block).
- Route to MED-2053.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 Prelims GS-I Q35 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2053, MED-2063.
**Trap:** Do not invent the key; do not attribute to Paes.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains route.
**Back:** *"Thirty-five: the women who wrestle, read, count and foretell."*

### Flashcard MED-2071 — PYQ route: 2023 Prelims Q48 (Tungabhadra dam and canal)
**Front:** What does 2023 Prelims Q48 test?
**Definition (learn):** Vijayanagara rulers and the Tungabhadra dam/canal construction (Deva Raya I).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: Deva Raya I's Tungabhadra dam and canal (✅ owner 09 PYQ block).
- Official key unavailable locally; answer not inferred (✅ owner 09 PYQ block).
- Route to MED-2043.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Prelims GS-I Q48 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2043, MED-2039.
**Trap:** Dam/canal is Deva Raya I, not Deva Raya II (✅ owner 09 §25).
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains route.
**Back:** *"Forty-eight: Deva I's dam feeds the doab."*

### Flashcard MED-2072 — PYQ route: 2024 Prelims Q56 (Bhatkal)
**Front:** What does 2024 Prelims Q56 test?
**Definition (learn):** The medieval ruler who allowed the Portuguese a fort at Bhatkal, with the official Set-A key available locally.
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: Bhatkal fort attribution (✅ owner 09 PYQ block).
- Answer not recorded in the owner (✅ owner 09 PYQ block).
- Route to MED-2052.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Prelims GS-I Q56 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** MED-2052, MED-2051.
**Trap:** Use Krishnadevaraya only with source caution (✅ owner 09 §4).
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains route.
**Back:** *"Fifty-six: Bhatkal is the Portuguese fort question."*

### Flashcard MED-2073 — Gesu Daraz and Sufi institutions in the Bahmani kingdom
**Front:** How did Bahmani rulers use Sufi and Islamic institutions?
**Definition (learn):** Bahmani patronage of mosques, madrasas and Sufi institutions, with Hindu officials and negotiation with non-Muslim chiefs.
**Breakdown:**
- Patronised mosques, madrasas and Sufi institutions (✅ owner 09 §22).
- Used Hindu officials and negotiated with non-Muslim chiefs (✅ owner 09 §22).
- Gesu Daraz and Ahmad Shah's saintly memory (✅ owner 09 §22). Doctrinal Sufi history belongs to the Bhakti-Sufi group (MED-2076 onward).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gesu Daraz (✅ owner 09 §13, §22).
**Links:** MED-2046, MED-2062.
**Trap:** Do not treat Sufi patronage as proof of uniform religious policy (✅ owner 09 §22).
**Mains hook:** Religious institutions mediated legitimacy; composite culture must be evidenced, not assumed.
**Back:** *"Gesu Daraz's memory serves the Bahmani court."*

### Flashcard MED-2074 — Ashtadiggajas and Telugu court tradition
**Front:** What is the Ashtadiggajas tradition in Vijayanagara's court?
**Definition (learn):** An important Telugu court tradition of eight poets associated with Krishnadevaraya's court, whose canonical roster needs source checking.
**Breakdown:**
- Telugu court tradition (✅ owner 09 §23).
- Later literary canonisation should not be read as a complete contemporary roster (✅ owner 09 §23).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Krishnadevaraya's court (⚠️ roster not given in owner).
**Links:** MED-2051, MED-2064.
**Trap:** Do not list members as verified contemporaries unless checked (✅ owner 09 §23).
**Mains hook:** Literary patronage as legitimacy; canon formation is a later process.
**Back:** *"Eight elephants of Telugu verse: later canon, not a roll call."*

### Flashcard MED-2075 — Vijayanagara religious patronage and Bahmani composite institutions
**Front:** How did Vijayanagara and Bahmani use religion to legitimise rule?
**Definition (learn):** Vijayanagara used temple patronage and titles; Bahmani used mosques, madrasas and Sufi institutions; both employed the other faith's personnel.
**Breakdown:**
- Vijayanagara: temple patronage and titles; supported different Hindu sects and employed Muslims (✅ owner 09 §22).
- Bahmani: mosques, madrasas, Sufi institutions; Hindu officials (✅ owner 09 §22).
- Temples, mosques and dargahs held property and mediated legitimacy (✅ owner 09 §22).
- Shared motifs do not prove harmony; war damage and patronage were event-specific (✅ owner 09 §22, §24).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2073, MED-2061, MED-2062.
**Trap:** "Composite culture" must be evidenced, not assumed harmony (✅ owner 09 §22).
**Mains hook:** Composite culture as evidenced interaction in language, craft, court, military or institutions.
**Back:** *"Temple for the Raya, mosque for the Sultan, both for the land."*

### GROUP — 10 The Bhakti & Sufi Movements

### Flashcard MED-2076 — Bhakti and Sufism as umbrella labels: symbiosis, not merger
**Front:** Why are "the Bhakti movement" and "the Sufi movement" treated as survey labels rather than single movements?
**Definition (learn):** Bhakti names diverse devotional relations to a deity; Sufism (tasawwuf) names Islamic mystical disciplines, orders and ethical training. Neither had one founder, organisation or programme.
**Breakdown:**
- Bhakti: diverse devotional relationships, practices, communities, texts and institutions (✅ owner 10 §1, §9).
- Sufism: Islamic mystical disciplines, ethical training, doctrines and orders (✅ owner 10 §9).
- Satish Chandra's frame: symbiosis of monotheism, guru/pir, mystical union and critique of empty ritual (✅ adv. 10 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not a single pan-Indian campaign (✅ owner 10 §9).
**Links:** MED-2121 (symbiosis), MED-2120 (trap), MED-2086 (Chishti popularity).
**Trap:** Similar vocabulary (love, devotion, guru/pir) does not mean identical theology or one-way borrowing (✅ adv. 10 §5).
**Mains hook:** Discuss Bhakti and Sufi movements as rapprochement, but note the limits of shared vocabulary.
**Back:** *"Umbrella, not uniform: shared words, separate theologies."*

### Flashcard MED-2077 — Sufi organisation: silsila, khanqah, pir and murid
**Front:** How were Sufi orders organised?
**Definition (learn):** Sufi groups formed silsilas (chains/orders) led by a pir (guide) in a khanqah (hospice), with disciples (murids) and successors (khalifas/walis).
**Breakdown:**
- Silsila: chain or order of spiritual transmission (✅ owner 10 §17).
- Pir/shaykh and murid: guide and disciple (✅ owner 10 §17).
- Khanqah: residence, hospice and teaching-service centre (✅ owner 10 §17).
- Khalifas/walis: successors (✅ owner 10 §1).
- Tariqa: disciplined path or order; not merely a "sect" (✅ owner 10 §17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Khanqahs needed food, buildings, labour, books, disciples and hospitality (✅ owner 10 §20).
**Links:** MED-2078, MED-2079, MED-2118 (khanqah economy).
**Trap:** Khanqah ≠ dargah. A khanqah is a living hospice; a dargah is a shrine developed after a saint's death (✅ owner 10 §17, §20).
**Mains hook:** The pir–murid link explains how Sufi authority moved through networks rather than through state office.
**Back:** *"Silsila chains, pir leads, murid follows, khanqah hosts."*

### Flashcard MED-2078 — Ba-shara and be-shara Sufis
**Front:** What is the difference between ba-shara and be-shara Sufis?
**Definition (learn):** Ba-shara orders followed Islamic law (shari'a); be-shara wandering saints (qalandars) were not bound by it.
**Breakdown:**
- Ba-shara: law-bound orders, including Chishti and Suhrawardi (✅ owner 10 §4; adv. 10 §2).
- Be-shara: wandering non-law-bound saints/qalandars (✅ adv. 10 §2).
- Caution: not all Sufis were liberal or non-conformist (⚠️ owner 10 §8.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2077, MED-2079, MED-2084.
**Trap:** Be-shara is not "anti-Islam"; the distinction concerns law-bound status, not belief in God.
**Mains hook:** Use ba-shara/be-shara to argue that Sufi variety cannot be summarised as a single liberal tradition.
**Back:** *"Ba-shara keeps the law; be-shara wanders outside it."*

### Flashcard MED-2079 — Muinuddin Chishti of Ajmer
**Front:** Who was Muinuddin Chishti and where did he settle?
**Definition (learn):** Founder-figure of the Chishti presence in India, who settled at Ajmer and whose death in 1235 anchors the order's early chronology.
**Breakdown:**
- Settled at Ajmer; death 1235 (✅ owner 10 mini-timeline; adv. 10 mini-timeline).
- Primary object: to help Muslims live a life of devotion, not to seek conversions (✅ adv. 10 §1).
- Chishti ideals: poverty, service, sama and distance from politics (✅ owner 10 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Arrival: c. 1192 (owner basic mini-timeline) vs 1206 (owner advanced mini-timeline, "after Turkish rule is firm there"). ⚠️ The owners differ on arrival. Death: owners give 1235 (owner §19: "1235/36 across chronologies"); Wikipedia (Moinuddin Chishti) gives 15 March 1236 (✅ wiki; death-year conflict flagged).
**Links:** MED-2080 (Bakhtiyar Kaki, Farid), MED-2081 (Nizamuddin), MED-2086.
**Trap:** Do not state the arrival year as a fixed fact; the owners give two dates (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** Chishti popularity rested partly on the Ajmer centre's distance from political office (✅ owner 10 §25 mains structure).
**Back:** *"Ajmer holds Muinuddin: arrives around 1192 or 1206, dies in 1235."*

### Flashcard MED-2080 — Bakhtiyar Kaki and Baba Farid (Chishti centres)
**Front:** Which centres do Qutbuddin Bakhtiyar Kaki and Baba Farid represent?
**Definition (learn):** Bakhtiyar Kaki established Chishti influence at Delhi; Baba Farid led the Chishti centre at Ajodhan.
**Breakdown:**
- Bakhtiyar Kaki: Chishti influence at Delhi (✅ owner 10 §4, §19).
- Baba Farid: Ajodhan centre; verses later preserved in Sikh scripture show cross-institutional movement (✅ owner 10 §19).
- Chishti centres: Ajmer–Muinuddin; Delhi–Kaki/Nizamuddin/Chiragh; Ajodhan–Farid; Gulbarga–Gesu Daraz (✅ owner 10 §24).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ajodhan (Pakpattan) is the Farid centre (⚠️ place name equivalence is standard knowledge, not stated in owner).
**Links:** MED-2079, MED-2081, MED-2083.
**Trap:** Baba Farid of Ajodhan is not the Delhi centre; keep the centre-saint pair explicit.
**Mains hook:** Use Farid's verses in Sikh scripture to show a Sufi voice entering another tradition's canon.
**Back:** *"Kaki holds Delhi, Farid holds Ajodhan: centres, not one mission."*

### Flashcard MED-2081 — Nizamuddin Auliya (d. 1325) and the Fawa'id al-Fu'ad
**Front:** What is known about Nizamuddin Auliya and what source records his teaching?
**Definition (learn):** The Delhi Chishti saint (d. 1325) whose long career survived dynastic upheavals; his conversations were recorded in the Fawa'id al-Fu'ad by Amir Hasan Sijzi.
**Breakdown:**
- Died 1325 (✅ owner 10 mini-timeline).
- Adopted yogic breathing; yogis called him a sidh (✅ owner 10 §4, §8.2).
- Remark that every community has its own path (✅ adv. 10 §1).
- Recorded in Fawa'id al-Fu'ad, near-contemporary by Amir Hasan Sijzi, not a verbatim modern transcript (✅ owner 10 §10).
- "Aloof from rulers" is a strong ideal, not total apoliticism (✅ owner 10 §19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1325 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** MED-2079, MED-2082, MED-2116 (source criticism).
**Trap:** "Chishtis were anti-society ascetics" is wrong (✅ adv. 10 §5). Nizamuddin served the needy and advised the public.
**Mains hook:** Nizamuddin shows technique borrowing (breath control) as cross-tradition exchange, but a single anecdote cannot prove systematic borrowing.
**Back:** *"Nizamuddin dies 1325; yogis call him sidh and Sijzi records the talk."*

### Flashcard MED-2082 — Nasiruddin Chiragh-i-Delhi (Delhi continuation)
**Front:** Who was Nasiruddin Chiragh-i-Delhi and where does he belong?
**Definition (learn):** A Chishti saint of the Delhi line, belonging to the Delhi continuation after Nizamuddin.
**Breakdown:**
- Member of the Delhi Chishti line (✅ owner 10 §1, §19).
- Died mid-fourteenth century (✅ owner 10 §19).
- Listed among Chishtis who stressed simplicity, poverty, service, sama and distance from politics (✅ owner 10 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mid-14th c. death (✅ owner 10 §19).
**Links:** MED-2081, MED-2080.
**Trap:** Do not confuse Nasiruddin Chiragh with Nizamuddin Auliya; the owner lists them as separate Delhi figures.
**Mains hook:** Delhi's Chishti network shows continuity across generations.
**Back:** *"Chiragh lights the Delhi line after Nizamuddin."*

### Flashcard MED-2083 — Gesu Daraz (d. 1422): Chishti in the Deccan
**Front:** How did Gesu Daraz carry Chishti life into the Deccan?
**Definition (learn):** A Chishti saint (d. 1422) who carried the order's institutional and literary life into the Deccan and remembered Gulbarga.
**Breakdown:**
- Died 1422 (✅ owner 10 §19).
- Gulbarga centre (✅ owner 10 §18, §24).
- Bahmani memory: Ahmad Shah I's saintly association (✅ owner 09 §22, cross-ref).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1422 (✅ owner 10 §19).
**Links:** MED-2080, MED-2073 (Bahmani Sufi patronage).
**Trap:** Gesu Daraz is the Gulbarga Chishti anchor, not a Delhi Chishti.
**Mains hook:** Shows regional Sufi institutions in the Bahmani state.
**Back:** *"Gesu Daraz: Gulbarga's Chishti, died 1422."*

### Flashcard MED-2084 — Suhrawardi order: Bahauddin Zakariya and Multan
**Front:** What is the Suhrawardi order and how did it differ from the Chishti order?
**Definition (learn):** A ba-shara Sufi order strong in Punjab and Multan, whose centres accepted grants, wealth and state contact more readily.
**Breakdown:**
- Strong in Punjab and Multan (✅ owner 10 §1).
- Bahauddin Zakariya at Multan and related Punjab/Sindh networks (✅ owner 10 §18).
- Accepted grants and some held official posts (✅ owner 10 §5).
- Shihabuddin Suhrawardi listed as a major Suhrawardi figure (✅ owner 10 §2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Multan centre (✅ owner 10 §19).
**Links:** MED-2085 (comparison), MED-2078, MED-2079.
**Trap:** "Suhrawardis rejected all state contact" is wrong (✅ owner 10 §5).
**Mains hook:** Patronage does not prove spiritual insincerity; the owner notes variation by centre and reign (✅ owner 10 §8.2 unit 2).
**Back:** *"Multan takes grants; Suhrawardi takes state contact."*

### Flashcard MED-2085 — Comparison pair: Chishti aloofness vs Suhrawardi grants
**Front:** Compare the Chishti and Suhrawardi approaches to the state.
**Definition (learn):** Two ba-shara orders with different political economies and state relations.
**Breakdown:**
- Chishti: poverty, aloofness from rulers, service, refusal of fixed office or grants as an ethical claim (✅ owner 10 §2, §20).
- Suhrawardi: grants, affluence and closer state contact (✅ adv. 10 §2).
- Both were not uniform; later shrines accumulated endowments (✅ owner 10 §20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2084, MED-2080, MED-2086.
**Trap:** Do not claim Chishtis refused everything; the owner says "practice varied" (✅ owner 10 §20).
**Mains hook:** Use the pair to show Sufi orders varied in political economy.
**Back:** *"Chishti refuse, Suhrawardi accept: both are ba-shara."*

### Flashcard MED-2086 — Why the Chishti order spread in the Ganga valley
**Front:** What explains the Chishti order's popularity in the Ganga valley?
**Definition (learn):** The Chishti appeal rested on four factors: vernacular accessibility, emotional devotion, service ethic and aloofness from state power.
**Breakdown:**
- Vernacular/Hindawi verses (✅ owner 10 §4, §8.1).
- Sama and emotional devotion (✅ owner 10 §8.1).
- Service and poverty (✅ owner 10 §8.1).
- Aloofness from state power, creating credibility across communities (✅ owner 10 §8.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ganga valley (✅ owner 10 §7).
**Links:** MED-2087 (sama, Hindawi), MED-2085, MED-2079.
**Trap:** Chishti aloofness is an ideal, not total apoliticism (✅ owner 10 §19).
**Mains hook:** Thesis: Chishti credibility came from vernacular access and service, not from conversion (✅ owner 10 §8.2 unit 1).
**Back:** *"Hindawi, sama, service, distance: four keys to the Ganga valley."*

### Flashcard MED-2087 — Sama and Hindawi verses in devotional practice
**Front:** What are sama and Hindawi verses in Chishti practice?
**Definition (learn):** Sama is devotional audition or music in particular settings, contested internally; Hindawi verses are vernacular devotional verses used for impact.
**Breakdown:**
- Sama: devotional audition/music in particular settings; contested internally (✅ owner 10 §17).
- Chishtis used sama and often Hindawi/Hindi verses for devotional impact (✅ owner 10 §4).
- Sama parallels kirtan and bhajan in Bhakti, but ritual rules and acceptability vary (✅ owner 10 §22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2086, MED-2101 (Chaitanya kirtan), MED-2076.
**Trap:** Sama is not the same as Bhakti kirtan; the rules differ.
**Mains hook:** Music and performance widened affective access but did not erase literacy or patronage (✅ owner 10 §16).
**Back:** *"Sama sings, Hindawi speaks: the Chishti voice."*

### Flashcard MED-2088 — Qadiri and Shattari orders
**Front:** What are the Qadiri and Shattari orders in the owner's matrix?
**Definition (learn):** Qadiri: flexible devotional practice with Mughal-era links, Punjab/Lahore networks. Shattari: rapid-path claims, court links and yogic engagement, with Muhammad Ghawth of Gwalior.
**Breakdown:**
- Qadiri: later-medieval/early-modern north India; flexible practices; Mughal-era links; internal diversity (✅ owner 10 §18).
- Shattari: fifteenth–sixteenth century; Muhammad Ghawth of Gwalior; rapid-path claims; court links (✅ owner 10 §18).
- Shattari: not proof of doctrinal merger (✅ owner 10 §18).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Fifteenth–sixteenth century (✅ owner 10 §18).
**Links:** MED-2089 (Naqshbandi), MED-2077.
**Trap:** Order labels identify lineages, not identical behaviour (✅ owner 10 §18).
**Mains hook:** Later Sufi orders show how Sufism responded to court and Mughal politics.
**Back:** *"Qadir flexes, Shattari rushes the path."*

### Flashcard MED-2089 — Naqshbandi and Ahmad Sirhindi (1564–1624)
**Front:** What is the Naqshbandi order's Indian centre and why is Ahmad Sirhindi an anachronism for Kabir?
**Definition (learn):** The Naqshbandi order emphasised silent dhikr and law/reform language in late sixteenth-century Delhi and north India, associated with Baqi Billah and Ahmad Sirhindi (1564–1624).
**Breakdown:**
- Silent dhikr often emphasised (✅ owner 10 §18).
- Strong law/reform language, not one uniform orthodox party (✅ owner 10 §18).
- Ahmad Sirhindi (1564–1624): Naqshbandi and Mughal-era debates; cannot influence fifteenth-century Kabir (✅ owner 10 §19).
- Wahdat al-shuhud linked to Sirhindi's critique (✅ owner 10 §17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sirhindi 1564–1624 (✅ owner 10 §19).
**Links:** MED-2090 (wahdat), MED-2100 (Kabir), MED-2088.
**Trap:** Kabir and Ahmad Sirhindi were not contemporaneous teacher and disciple (✅ owner 10 §24). Nimbarka is not a Sirhindi disciple (✅ owner 10 §24).
**Mains hook:** Use Sirhindi to show Mughal-era reform debates rather than fifteenth-century Bhakti.
**Back:** *"Sirhindi: sixteenth-century Naqshbandi, not Kabir's teacher."*

### Flashcard MED-2090 — Wahdat al-wujud and wahdat al-shuhud
**Front:** How should the two Sufi unity doctrines be stated?
**Definition (learn):** Wahdat al-wujud ("unity of being") is associated with Ibn Arabi; wahdat al-shuhud ("unity of witnessing") is linked to Ahmad Sirhindi's critique and reformulation.
**Breakdown:**
- Wahdat al-wujud: "unity of being", associated with Ibn Arabi; "pantheism" can mislead (✅ owner 10 §17).
- Wahdat al-shuhud: "unity of witnessing", linked to Sirhindi (✅ owner 10 §17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ibn Arabi and Sirhindi (✅ owner 10 §17).
**Links:** MED-2089, MED-2091 (fana).
**Trap:** Do not translate wahdat al-wujud as "pantheism" without caution (✅ owner 10 §17).
**Mains hook:** Doctrinal differences inside Sufism show that theology was never one Sufi system.
**Back:** *"Being is one, witnessing is one; Sirhindi reframes the witness."*

### Flashcard MED-2091 — Fana, baqa and Mansur bin Hallaj's "Anal-Haq"
**Front:** What are fana and baqa, and why does Mansur bin Hallaj matter?
**Definition (learn):** Fana is effacement of the ego; baqa is abiding in or with God. Mansur bin Hallaj's "Anal-Haq" (I am the Truth) episode symbolises the tension between Sufi ecstasy and orthodoxy; he was executed in the tenth century.
**Breakdown:**
- Fana: effacement of the ego (✅ owner 10 §17; adv. 10 §4).
- Baqa: abiding in or with God; translations are approximate (✅ owner 10 §17).
- Mansur bin Hallaj executed; martyrdom and ecstatic union become symbolic (✅ adv. 10 mini-timeline).
- Rabia stressed disinterested love (8th c.) (✅ adv. 10 mini-timeline).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mansur executed 10th c. (✅ adv. 10). Rabia 8th c. (✅ adv. 10). The phrase "I am the Truth" is ⚠️ standard translation.
**Links:** MED-2090, MED-2076.
**Trap:** Translations of fana and baqa are approximate; do not give them as exact dictionary equivalents.
**Mains hook:** Mystical tension with orthodoxy as a source of martyrdom narratives.
**Back:** *"Fana dissolves the self; Mansur's 'Anal-Haq' cost him his life."*

### Flashcard MED-2092 — Alvars and Nayanars: early southern bridge (c. sixth–ninth century)
**Front:** What did the Tamil Alvars and Nayanars contribute to Bhakti?
**Definition (learn):** Vaishnava Alvars and Shaiva Nayanars whose hymns, pilgrimage and temple networks in the Tamil south formed early southern Bhakti.
**Breakdown:**
- c. sixth–ninth century, Tamil south (✅ owner 10 §11).
- Hymn, pilgrimage and temple networks (✅ owner 10 §11).
- Egalitarian features among Nayanars and Alvars, later moderated by temple ritual and Brahmanical authority (✅ adv. 10 §1).
- Bridge from ancient/early-medieval owners (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Alvars (Vaishnava), Nayanars (Shaiva) (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Links:** MED-2093 (Ramanuja), MED-2094 (Nimbarka), MED-2076.
**Trap:** Do not place Alvars and Nayanars in the 14th century; they are earlier, bridging owners (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Mains hook:** The south's egalitarian moment was later moderated; a case for "change and limit".
**Back:** *"Alvars and Nayanars: sixth to ninth, hymn and temple first."*

### Flashcard MED-2093 — Ramanuja and Vishishtadvaita (11th–12th century)
**Front:** What is Ramanuja's doctrine and why does it matter for Bhakti access?
**Definition (learn):** Ramanuja's qualified non-dualism (Vishishtadvaita) with surrender (prapatti) as the path, founding Sri Vaishnava institutions.
**Breakdown:**
- Vishishtadvaita: qualified non-dualism (✅ owner 10 §12).
- Prapatti: surrender (✅ owner 10 §12).
- Opened bhakti to all castes, bridging popular devotion and Vedic tradition (✅ adv. 10 §1).
- Chronology: eleventh–twelfth century (✅ owner 10 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Eleventh–twelfth century (✅ owner 10 §12).
**Links:** MED-2094 (Nimbarka), MED-2095 (Madhva), MED-2096 (Vallabha).
**Trap:** Access ideals and later institutional practice are not identical (✅ owner 10 §12). Ramanuja ≠ Madhva; Madhva = Dvaita.
**Mains hook:** Ramanuja shows that Vedanta could carry devotional egalitarianism, yet temple authority remained.
**Back:** *"Ramanuja: qualified non-dual, surrender-led, caste-open in principle."*

### Flashcard MED-2094 — Nimbarka: Dvaitadvaita / Bhedabheda (PYQ 2019 Q13)
**Front:** What is Nimbarka's doctrine and what is the chronology trap?
**Definition (learn):** Nimbarka's dualistic non-dualism (Dvaitadvaita or Bhedabheda) with Radha–Krishna devotion, dated c. 12th–13th century (disputed).
**Breakdown:**
- Dvaitadvaita / Bhedabheda (✅ owner 10 §4, §12).
- Radha–Krishna devotion (✅ owner 10 §4).
- Chronology disputed; commonly before the fifteenth-century expansion (✅ owner 10 §12).
- Distinct from Ramanuja's Vishishtadvaita and Madhva's Dvaita (✅ owner 10 §4).
- PYQ 2019 Prelims Q13 routes Nimbarka and Kabir chronology; answer not inferred (✅ owner 10 §24).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 12th–13th century; date disputed (✅ owner 10 §4).
**Links:** MED-2093, MED-2095, MED-2112 (PYQ route).
**Trap:** Nimbarka = Dvaitadvaita/Bhedabheda, not a Mughal-era disciple of Sirhindi (✅ owner 10 §24).
**Mains hook:** Doctrine safer than exact date; use Nimbarka to show pre-15th-century Vaishnava theological roots.
**Back:** *"Nimbarka: dual non-dual, Radha-Krishna, date disputed."*

### Flashcard MED-2095 — Madhva and Dvaita; Udupi lineage
**Front:** What is Madhva's doctrine and lineage?
**Definition (learn):** Madhva's dualism (Dvaita) with Vishnu devotion and an Udupi-centred lineage, dated to the thirteenth century.
**Breakdown:**
- Dvaita (dualism) (✅ owner 10 §12).
- Vishnu devotion; Udupi-centred lineage (✅ owner 10 §12).
- Thirteenth century (✅ owner 10 §12).
- Not part of one Ramanuja school (✅ owner 10 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13th century (✅ owner 10 §12).
**Links:** MED-2093, MED-2094.
**Trap:** Madhva ≠ Ramanuja's school; Dvaita ≠ Vishishtadvaita (✅ owner 10 §24).
**Mains hook:** Vaishnava Vedanta offers three paths (Ramanuja, Madhva, Nimbarka) and not one sequence (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Back:** *"Madhva: Dvaita from Udupi, not Ramanuja's school."*

### Flashcard MED-2096 — Vallabha: Shuddhadvaita and Pushtimarg
**Front:** What is Vallabha's doctrine and path?
**Definition (learn):** Vallabha's pure non-dualism (Shuddhadvaita) and grace-based path of nourishment (Pushtimarg), late fifteenth to early sixteenth century.
**Breakdown:**
- Shuddhadvaita: pure non-dualism (✅ owner 10 §12).
- Pushtimarg: grace/path of nourishment (✅ owner 10 §12).
- Late fifteenth–early sixteenth century (✅ owner 10 §12).
- Sect institutions developed historically (✅ owner 10 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Late 15th–early 16th century (✅ owner 10 §12).
**Links:** MED-2093, MED-2103 (Surdas).
**Trap:** Vallabha = Shuddhadvaita/Pushtimarg (✅ owner 10 §24).
**Mains hook:** Sect institutions develop over time; do not project later practice to the founder.
**Back:** *"Vallabha: pure non-dual, grace-fed path."*

### Flashcard MED-2097 — Ramananda: Rama devotion and disciples Ravidas, Kabir, Sena, Sadhana
**Front:** What do the sources say about Ramananda's teaching and disciples?
**Definition (learn):** The conventional fourteenth–fifteenth century teacher of Rama devotion whose traditions opened devotion across caste boundaries.
**Breakdown:**
- Rama devotion and wider vernacular access (✅ owner 10 §12).
