- Disciples: Ravidas, Kabir, Sena, Sadhana (✅ owner 10 §1, §4).
- Precise disciple chronology not equally secure (✅ owner 10 §1, §8.2 unit 5).
- Disciple lists are hagiographically mediated (✅ owner 10 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Conventional 14th–15th century (✅ owner 10 §12).
**Links:** MED-2098 (Kabir), MED-2099 (Nanak), MED-2116 (hagiography).
**Trap:** Do not state the disciple list as verified; present it as tradition (✅ adv. 10 §1).
**Mains hook:** Ramananda's movement is the cross-caste route, but the evidence is hagiographic.
**Back:** *"Ramananda's list: Ravidas, Kabir, Sena, Sadhana: tradition, not roll call."*

### Flashcard MED-2098 — Kabir: one God, critique of ritual and caste
**Front:** What did Kabir teach and what limits does the owner place on him?
**Definition (learn):** The nirguna saint of the Banaras artisan milieu, who emphasised one God and denounced idol worship, pilgrimages, ritual formalism and caste discrimination.
**Breakdown:**
- One God; denounced idol worship, pilgrimages, ritual formalism and caste (✅ owner 10 §1).
- Banaras/artisan milieu; nirguna attributed corpus (✅ owner 10 §13).
- Corpus layers: Bijak, Granthavali and Adi Granth differ (✅ owner 10 §13).
- Not a social reformer in the modern sense; stayed outside royal courts (✅ adv. 10 §1; owner §5).
- Kabir's climate of opinion, not immediate social collapse (✅ adv. 10 §2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dates not stated in owner; ⚠️ do not assign a birth or death year without checking.
**Links:** MED-2097, MED-2100 (Kabir–Nanak comparison), MED-2112 (PYQ 2019 chronology route), MED-2113.
**Trap:** Kabir was not a state-backed reformer, and his verses were not textually fixed from his lifetime (✅ owner 10 §5; adv. 10 §5).
**Mains hook:** Kabir's critique and the limits of the reform (✅ owner 10 §7).
**Back:** *"Kabir: one God, no idols, no caste; oral verses, later written."*

### Flashcard MED-2099 — Guru Nanak: one God, guru, householder life (1469–1539)
**Front:** What did Guru Nanak teach and what are his dates?
**Definition (learn):** The founder-figure of the Sikh tradition (born 1469 at Talwandi/Nankana on the Ravi), who taught one God, purity of character, guru's guidance, household life and rejection of empty ritual.
**Breakdown:**
- Born 1469 at Talwandi/Nankana on the Ravi (✅ owner 10 mini-timeline).
- Taught remembrance of the divine name, ethical work, sharing, guru-guided discipline and household life (✅ owner 10 §14).
- Did not intend to found a new religion; his ideas later gave birth to Sikhism (✅ owner 10 §4).
- Sangat and langar became community practices; forms developed over time (✅ owner 10 §14).
- Not a mixture of Hinduism and Islam (✅ owner 10 §14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Born 1469 (✅ owner 10). Death: 1539 in standard Sikh chronology (✅ owner 10 §14); Part I prints 1538 (⚠️ owner conflict flagged).
**Links:** MED-2100 (Kabir–Nanak pair), MED-2097, MED-2116 (Janamsakhis).
**Trap:** "Nanak preached renunciation of household life" is wrong; he advocated a middle path (✅ owner 10 §5).
**Mains hook:** Nanak's householder spirituality as a distinct strand in the sixteenth century (✅ owner 10 §8.2 unit 4).
**Back:** *"Nanak 1469–1539: one God, guru, home, and langar, not a new faith by design."*

### Flashcard MED-2100 — Comparison pair: Kabir and Nanak
**Front:** Compare Kabir and Nanak on one God, ritual and social equality.
**Definition (learn):** Two nirguna-leaning saints who both criticised ritual and caste while stressing one reality and ethical life.
**Breakdown:**
- Both: one God; criticism of empty ritual and caste distinction (✅ owner 10 §8.1).
- Kabir: attacked ritual leadership of both communities; outside royal courts (✅ adv. 10 §4).
- Nanak: householder life; guru's guidance; community practice (✅ owner 10 §8.2 unit 4).
- Differences: remembered social milieu, teaching vocabulary and institutional legacy (✅ owner 10 §8.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Nanak 1469–1539 (✅ owner 10). Kabir: dates not stated (⚠️).
**Links:** MED-2098, MED-2099.
**Trap:** Nirguna does not automatically mean socially radical; saguna does not automatically mean conservative (✅ owner 10 §12).
**Mains hook:** Compare the two on doctrine, practice and institutional legacy, not on a single "reform" scale.
**Back:** *"Kabir critiques from outside; Nanak builds a household and a langar."*

### Flashcard MED-2101 — Chaitanya (1486–1533): ecstatic kirtan in Bengal and Puri (PYQ 2018 Q11)
**Front:** What did Chaitanya do and how does it route to the 2018 GS-I Q11?
**Definition (learn):** Chaitanya (1486–1533) energised Krishna-bhakti in Bengal and Puri through ecstatic congregational singing (kirtan).
**Breakdown:**
- Ecstatic kirtan in Bengal and Puri (✅ owner 10 §1, §4).
- Made devotion emotionally and publicly accessible (✅ owner 10 §1).
- Gaudiya Krishna devotion (✅ owner 10 §12).
- 2018 GS-I Q11 asks the re-orientation of Bhakti under Chaitanya (✅ adv. 10 PYQ block; Mains, 15 marks, 250 words).
- Owner: kirtan widened devotional access more than it transformed social structure (✅ owner 10 §8.2 unit 9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1486–1533 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** MED-2087 (sama vs kirtan), MED-2113 (PYQ route), MED-2103 (Surdas).
**Trap:** Later theology and biography cannot all be projected onto one lifetime (✅ owner 10 §12).
**Mains hook:** "Chaitanya revitalised Vaishnava devotion through kirtan and widened access, with limits on institutional reform" (✅ owner 10 §8.3).
**Back:** *"Chaitanya sings Krishna out loud: 1486 to 1533, Bengal and Puri."*

### Flashcard MED-2102 — Tulsidas and the Awadhi Ramcharitmanas
**Front:** What did Tulsidas's Ramcharitmanas do for Rama devotion?
**Definition (learn):** The Awadhi Ramcharitmanas made Rama devotion accessible to Hindi-speaking masses outside Sanskrit learning.
**Breakdown:**
- Awadhi language (✅ owner 10 §8.3 unit 2).
- Deep vernacular reach; devotional rather than anti-caste politics (✅ owner 10 §8.3).
- Sixteenth–seventeenth century (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sixteenth–seventeenth century devotional continuity (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Links:** MED-2103 (Surdas), MED-2119 (vernacularisation limit).
**Trap:** Ramcharitmanas is not a uniform anti-caste programme (✅ owner 10 §13).
**Mains hook:** Vernacular democratisation without full social transformation (✅ owner 10 §8.3 verdict).
**Back:** *"Tulsi's Awadhi: Rama for the masses, not a caste manifesto."*

### Flashcard MED-2103 — Surdas and Braj Krishna lyrics
**Front:** What did Surdas contribute to Krishna devotion?
**Definition (learn):** Braj Bhasha Krishna lyrics, refined literary art and popular devotion together; the Sursagar grew through transmission.
**Breakdown:**
- Krishna lyrics in Braj Bhasha (✅ owner 10 §8.3 unit 3).
- Literary and popular devotion together (✅ owner 10 §8.3).
- Sursagar grew through transmission (✅ owner 10 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further in owner.
**Links:** MED-2101, MED-2104 (Meera), MED-2096 (Vallabha).
**Trap:** The Sursagar's exact corpus cannot be read as a single authored text (✅ owner 10 §13).
**Mains hook:** Literary contribution strongest in emotional and aesthetic expression.
**Back:** *"Surdas sings Krishna in Braj: art and devotion together."*

### Flashcard MED-2104 — Meera and female devotional agency
**Front:** What does Meera's Krishna devotion show about gender and agency?
**Definition (learn):** Rajasthani/Hindi Krishna devotion of Meera, showing female agency and an individual devotee's challenge to social restraint.
**Breakdown:**
- Rajasthan/Gujarat associated; Krishna devotion (✅ owner 10 §13).
- Female devotional agency (✅ owner 10 §8.3 unit 4).
- Attributed poem corpus has layers (✅ owner 10 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None in owner.
**Links:** MED-2108 (Lalla), MED-2109 (Akka Mahadevi), MED-2118.
**Trap:** Spiritual agency did not automatically dismantle patriarchy (✅ owner 10 §8.3 unit 4).
**Mains hook:** Use Meera and other women mystics to test "social reform" claims carefully.
**Back:** *"Meera's agency: Krishna's voice, not a patriarchal exit."*

### Flashcard MED-2105 — Eknath, Tukaram and Marathi Bhakti (Varkari)
**Front:** What did Eknath and Tukaram contribute to Marathi Bhakti?
**Definition (learn):** Marathi vernacular devotional literature and Varkari continuity extended powerfully into the Deccan.
**Breakdown:**
- Marathi Bhakti in the Deccan (✅ owner 10 §8.3 unit 5).
- Varkari continuity, social critique and regional literature (✅ owner 10 §13).
- Namdev (Maharashtra; Varkari/Pandharpur currents) (✅ owner 10 §11, §13).
- Thirteenth–fourteenth century Namdev/Varkari currents (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Namdev 13th–14th c. (✅ owner 10 §11). Eknath/Tukaram sixteenth–seventeenth century (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Links:** MED-2106 (Gujarat), MED-2107 (Assam), MED-2102.
**Trap:** Regional languages flourished, but doctrinal systems remained diverse (✅ owner 10 §8.3 unit 5).
**Mains hook:** Use Marathi Bhakti for the Deccan route of vernacularisation.
**Back:** *"Namdev starts, Eknath and Tukaram carry Varkari to the Deccan."*

### Flashcard MED-2106 — Narsinha Mehta and Gujarati Bhakti
**Front:** What is Narsinha Mehta's place in Bhakti?
**Definition (learn):** The Gujarati devotional poet whose Krishna idiom widened Bhakti's regional social reach.
**Breakdown:**
- Gujarati Bhakti; Krishna idiom (✅ owner 10 §8.3 unit 6; §13).
- Linguistic and emotional integration more than formal theology (✅ owner 10 §8.3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None in owner.
**Links:** MED-2105, MED-2103.
**Trap:** Cultural integration in Gujarat was linguistic, not formal theology.
**Mains hook:** Use Narsinha Mehta as a Gujarat example in a 15-mark literature answer (✅ owner 10 §8.3).
**Back:** *"Narsinha: Gujarati Krishna song, widened reach."*

### Flashcard MED-2107 — Shankaradeva and Eka Sarana Nama Dharma in Assam
**Front:** What institutions and doctrine does Shankaradeva's Assam Bhakti use?
**Definition (learn):** Eka Sarana Nama Dharma, a fifteenth–sixteenth century Assamese Bhakti built on performance, namghar and sattra networks.
**Breakdown:**
- Eka Sarana Nama Dharma (✅ owner 10 §11, §13).
- Namghar and sattra institutions (✅ owner 10 §11, §13).
- Performance (✅ owner 10 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Fifteenth–sixteenth century (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Links:** MED-2024 (Ahom contrast), MED-2109 (Akka Mahadevi).
**Trap:** Full Assam political history stays in the Ahom owner boundary; Shankaradeva is religious-institutional (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Mains hook:** Assam shows Bhakti institutions beyond north India.
**Back:** *"Shankaradeva: one name, sattra and namghar, Assam's Bhakti."*

### Flashcard MED-2108 — Lalla (Lal Ded) and the vakhs of Kashmir
**Front:** Who was Lalla (Lal Ded) and what is her literary form?
**Definition (learn):** The fourteenth-century Kashmiri woman mystic whose vakhs (sayings) form regional oral-literary memory.
**Breakdown:**
- Fourteenth-century Kashmir (✅ owner 10 §13).
- Vakhs (✅ owner 10 §13).
- Remembered Shaiva-mystical voice (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Fourteenth century (✅ owner 10 §13).
**Links:** MED-2104, MED-2116 (source criticism).
**Trap:** Exact corpus cautioned (✅ owner 10 §13).
**Mains hook:** Women's devotional agency across regions.
**Back:** *"Lalla's vakhs: Kashmiri oral mysticism, corpus cautioned."*

### Flashcard MED-2109 — Akka Mahadevi and Virashaiva vachanas (12th-century Karnataka)
**Front:** What is the Virashaiva (Lingayat) and vachana tradition and who is Akka Mahadevi?
**Definition (learn):** The twelfth-century Karnataka Shaiva social critique in vachana verse, including the woman poet Akka Mahadevi.
**Breakdown:**
- Twelfth-century Karnataka (✅ owner 10 §11).
- Virashaiva/Lingayat and vachana worlds (✅ owner 10 §11).
- Akka Mahadevi: women's devotional authorship (✅ owner 10 §15).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Twelfth century (✅ owner 10 §11).
**Links:** MED-2104, MED-2108.
**Trap:** Virashaiva ≠ Vaishnava Bhakti; keep Shaiva critique separate.
**Mains hook:** Women's devotional authorship shows agency under different conditions (✅ owner 10 §15).
**Back:** *"Akka and vachana: Karnataka's twelfth-century Shaiva voice."*

### Flashcard MED-2110 — Dadu Dayal and the Dadu Panth (16th century)
**Front:** What is Dadu Dayal's non-sectarian teaching and institutional legacy?
**Definition (learn):** The sixteenth-century Rajasthan nirguna teacher who taught non-sectarianism (nipakh) and whose followers formed the Dadu Panth.
**Breakdown:**
- Sixteenth-century Rajasthan; nirguna/Dadu Panth (✅ owner 10 §13).
- Non-sectarian (nipakh) teaching (✅ owner 10 §13).
- Later institutionalisation (✅ owner 10 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sixteenth century (✅ owner 10 §13).
**Links:** MED-2098 (Kabir), MED-2089.
**Trap:** Dadu is not Kabir's disciple in the owner; do not invent a lineage.
**Mains hook:** Non-sectarian teaching later hardened into a panth.
**Back:** *"Dadu: nipakh, non-sectarian, then a panth."*

### Flashcard MED-2111 — Kulah-Daran: PYQ 2022 Q58 trap
**Front:** What is Kulah-Daran and what is the PYQ 2022 Q58 route?
**Definition (learn):** "Cap-wearers" (Kulah-Daran) associated with Sayyid nobles or rulers in later historical writing; not a Bhakti sect or Sufi order.
**Breakdown:**
- Linked to Sayyid nobles/rulers in later historical writing (✅ owner 10 §8.2 unit 8).
- Provenance weak in primary-source usage (✅ owner 10 §8.2).
- Factual political owner is Topic 06 Sayyid history (✅ owner 10 §9).
- PYQ 2022 GS Prelims Q58 routes the identity question; answer not inferred (✅ owner 10 §24).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sayyid phase (✅ owner 10 §4).
**Links:** MED-2112 (PYQ 2019 chronology route), MED-2113 (Mains routes).
**Trap:** Kulah-Daran is not a Bhakti sect or Sufi order (✅ owner 10 §24).
**Mains hook:** Identity terms need source caution.
**Back:** *"Kulah-Daran: Sayyid-era cap-wearers, not a sect."*

### Flashcard MED-2112 — PYQ 2019 Q13: Nimbarka and Kabir chronology
**Front:** What does 2019 Prelims Q13 test?
**Definition (learn):** Bhakti saints Nimbarka and Kabir historical context.
**Breakdown:**
- Routes Nimbarka's chronology and Kabir's place (✅ owner 10 §24).
- Official key unavailable locally; answer not inferred (✅ owner 10 PYQ block).
- Use the chronology and doctrine caution from MED-2094 and MED-2098.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Prelims GS-I Q13 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** MED-2094, MED-2098.
**Trap:** Nimbarka is not a Sirhindi disciple, and Kabir and Sirhindi were not contemporaries (✅ owner 10 §24).
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains route.
**Back:** *"Thirteen: Nimbarka early, Kabir fifteenth-century, Sirhindi later."*

### Flashcard MED-2113 — PYQ 2018 GS-I Q11 and 2021 GS-I Q1: Bhakti Mains routes
**Front:** Which two Mains questions route to this topic and what do they demand?
**Definition (learn):** 2018 Q11: re-orientation of Bhakti under Chaitanya Mahaprabhu (15 marks, 250 words). 2021 Q1: nature and contribution of Bhakti literature (10 marks, 150 words).
**Breakdown:**
- 2018 GS-I Q11, Discuss, 15 marks, 250 words (✅ owner 10 PYQ block).
- 2021 GS-I Q1, Evaluate, 10 marks, 150 words (✅ owner 10 PYQ block).
- Each needs context, dimensions, evidence, counterpoint and conclusion (✅ owner 10 PYQ block).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Q11, 2021 Q1 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** MED-2101 (Chaitanya), MED-2114 (literature thesis).
**Trap:** Do not answer the 2021 question with only Chaitanya; literature is required (✅ owner 10 §8.3).
**Mains hook:** Use the two Mains routes to test both reorientation and literary contribution.
**Back:** *"2018 Chaitanya at fifteen marks, 2021 literature at ten."*

### Flashcard MED-2114 — Mains thesis: Bhakti literature as vernacular democratisation
**Front:** What is the owner's thesis for Bhakti literature (2021 Q1 style)?
**Definition (learn):** Bhakti literature's principal contribution was linguistic democratisation and emotional accessibility, creating shared cultural vocabularies while leaving many social structures intact.
**Breakdown:**
- Strands: Chaitanya's kirtan; Tulsidas; Surdas; Meera; Eknath/Tukaram; Narsinha Mehta (✅ owner 10 §8.3).
- Limits: much devotional rather than reform; caste persisted (✅ owner 10 §8.3 variation).
- Verdict: vernacular democratisation with social limits (✅ owner 10 §8.3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2113, MED-2119.
**Trap:** "Bhakti literature abolished caste" is wrong (✅ owner 10 §8.3 variation).
**Mains hook:** Structure a 10-mark answer on a single named literary example plus limit.
**Back:** *"Language opened the door; caste stayed in the house."*

### Flashcard MED-2115 — Bhakti-Sufi encounter without a merger formula
**Front:** What overlaps between Bhakti and Sufism can be named, and what boundaries remain?
**Definition (learn):** Overlaps: love/devotion and remembrance; guru/pir and disciple; song (kirtan/bhajan/sama); vernacular idioms; caste/ritual criticism by some figures; shared urban and pilgrimage spaces.
**Breakdown:**
- Love/devotion and remembrance: divine incarnation and revelation differ (✅ owner 10 §22).
- Guru/pir and disciple: lineage authority differs (✅ owner 10 §22).
- Song: ritual rules and acceptability vary (✅ owner 10 §22).
- Vernacular: Persian/Arabic/Sanskrit canons remain important (✅ owner 10 §22).
- Shared spaces: temple, matha, khanqah, dargah and panth not interchangeable (✅ owner 10 §22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2076, MED-2121.
**Trap:** "Syncretism" alone is not an explanation (✅ owner 10 §22).
**Mains hook:** Use "encounter", "symbiosis" or "shared vocabulary" only when a named source supports it.
**Back:** *"Share the words, keep the theology."*

### Flashcard MED-2116 — Source criticism: hagiographies, malfuzat and janamsakhis
**Front:** Which sources for Bhakti and Sufism need the strongest caution?
**Definition (learn):** Hagiographies, malfuzat, Fawa'id al-Fu'ad, tazkiras, janamsakhis, manuscript compilations and oral traditions, each with its own limit.
**Breakdown:**
- Hagiographies: ideals, miracles, lineage; not literal biography (✅ owner 10 §10).
- Malfuzat: recorded Sufi conversations; compiler and editing mediate (✅ owner 10 §10).
- Fawa'id al-Fu'ad: Amir Hasan Sijzi's record of Nizamuddin, not verbatim (✅ owner 10 §10).
- Tazkiras and manaqib: order memory and virtues, retrospective (✅ owner 10 §10).
- Janamsakhis: early Sikh memory, multiple recensions (✅ owner 10 §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sijzi's Fawa'id al-Fu'ad (✅ owner 10 §10).
**Links:** MED-2081, MED-2099, MED-2097.
**Trap:** Do not read hagiography as dated attendance registers (✅ owner 10 §10).
**Mains hook:** Method: source → date/genre → evidence → inference → limit (✅ owner 10 §10).
**Back:** *"Malfuzat talks, hagiography miracles, janamsakhi journeys: all need a limit."*

### Flashcard MED-2117 — Khanqah economy: futuh, waqf and dargah formation
**Front:** How did khanqahs and dargahs get resources, and how did a dargah develop?
**Definition (learn):** Khanqahs needed food and buildings; resources came from futuh (voluntary gifts), waqf (endowments), land grants, patrons and disciples. A dargah formed through burial, successor management, pilgrimage, donations and patronage after death.
**Breakdown:**
- Resources: futuh, waqf, land grants, patrons, disciples (✅ owner 10 §20).
- Chishti refusal of fixed office or grants: an ethical claim, but practice varied (✅ owner 10 §20).
- Dargah: developed after death through burial, management, pilgrimage, donations, architecture and political patronage (✅ owner 10 §20).
- Later shrine ritual may differ from founder's teaching (✅ owner 10 §20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Futuh; waqf (✅ owner 10 §20).
**Links:** MED-2077, MED-2085, MED-2116.
**Trap:** Khanqah and dargah are not the same institution (✅ owner 10 §17).
**Mains hook:** Institutionalisation of Sufi charisma into endowments and shrine economy.
**Back:** *"Futuh and waqf feed the khanqah; burial and pilgrimage make the dargah."*

### Flashcard MED-2118 — Conversion cannot be assigned mainly to saints
**Front:** What drove conversion in the medieval period, according to the owner?
**Definition (learn):** Conversion was not mainly the work of saints; agrarian settlement, marriage, patronage, social mobility, local conflict, persuasion, state context and community networks varied regionally.
**Breakdown:**
- Chishtis were "hardly interested in effecting conversions" (✅ owner 10 §8.2 unit 1).
- Causes: agrarian settlement, marriage, patronage, occupational/social mobility, local conflict, persuasion, state context and community networks (✅ owner 10 §21).
- Shared participation at shrines does not mean identical theology or permanent harmony (✅ owner 10 §21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2079, MED-2086, MED-2120.
**Trap:** "Chishtis mainly aimed at conversions" is wrong (✅ owner 10 §5).
**Mains hook:** Conversion needs regional and multi-causal explanation, not a saint-led narrative.
**Back:** *"Saints didn't convert; land, marriage, patronage and state did."*

### Flashcard MED-2119 — Vernacularisation and performance: how Bhakti literature developed
**Front:** What chain of development did Bhakti literature follow?
**Definition (learn):** From oral/aural language through song, kirtan, bhajan and storytelling to pilgrimage networks, manuscript compilation and a regional literary public.
**Breakdown:**
- Oral/aural language → song, recitation, kirtan, bhajan, storytelling (✅ owner 10 §16).
- Pilgrimage and temple/matha/sattra/panth networks (✅ owner 10 §16).
- Manuscript compilation and commentary; regional literary public and later canon (✅ owner 10 §16).
- Languages: Tamil hymns, Kannada vachanas, Marathi abhangs, Kashmiri vakhs, Braj lyric, Awadhi narrative, Bengali/Assamese song-drama (✅ owner 10 §16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2114, MED-2107, MED-2109.
**Trap:** Music widened access but did not erase literacy, patronage or caste (✅ owner 10 §16).
**Mains hook:** Use the chain to structure a 20-mark literary answer.
**Back:** *"Oral to song to pilgrimage to manuscript to canon."*

### Flashcard MED-2120 — Trap: Bhakti is not a reaction, Chishti is not apolitical
**Front:** What are the reaction and apoliticism traps?
**Definition (learn):** Bhakti cannot be reduced to a reaction against Islam or Brahmanism; Chishti aloofness from politics was an ideal, not total apoliticism.
**Breakdown:**
- Bhakti is not a reaction against Islam or Brahmanism alone (✅ owner 10 §9).
- Chishti refusal of office did not mean political irrelevance (✅ owner 10 §21).
- "Suhrawardis rejected all state contact" is wrong (✅ owner 10 §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2085, MED-2118.
**Trap:** Use "bounded engagement", not "total withdrawal".
**Mains hook:** Refusal does not prove irrelevance; patronage does not prove collaboration (✅ owner 10 §21).
**Back:** *"Not against, not apart: bounded engagement."*

### Flashcard MED-2121 — Advanced: symbiosis, not simple borrowing
**Front:** What is Satish Chandra's symbiosis frame for Bhakti and Sufism?
**Definition (learn):** The advanced owner warns against a defensive reaction reading and against direct one-way borrowing; common elements received renewed emphasis.
**Breakdown:**
- Common elements: monotheism, guru/pir, mystical union, critique of empty ritual (✅ adv. 10 §1).
- Not defensive reaction against Islam, not simple borrowing from Sufism (✅ adv. 10 §1).
- Yogic/Buddhist practices influenced Sufi forms; core ideology traced to Quran (✅ adv. 10 §2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2076, MED-2115.
**Trap:** Similar vocabulary ≠ identical theology (✅ adv. 10 §5).
**Mains hook:** Symbiosis as the Mains thesis; avoid mechanical causation (✅ adv. 10 §2).
**Back:** *"Symbiosis: shared roots, two branches."*

### Flashcard MED-2122 — Advanced: Bhakti's northern rise
**Front:** What enabled Bhakti's rise in the north?
**Definition (learn):** A weakened Rajput-Brahman order and vernacular devotional currents enabled the northern expansion of Bhakti.
**Breakdown:**
- Weakened Rajput-Brahman order (✅ adv. 10 §2).
- Vernacular devotional currents (✅ adv. 10 §2).
- Mass phenomenon mainly in the 14th–15th centuries (✅ adv. 10 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 14th–15th centuries (✅ adv. 10 §4).
**Links:** MED-2097, MED-2099.
**Trap:** Do not claim one movement mechanically caused the other (✅ adv. 10 §2).
**Mains hook:** Northern Bhakti's rise was multi-causal.
**Back:** *"Weak order, vernacular song: north Bhakti rises in the fourteenth and fifteenth."*

### Flashcard MED-2123 — Advanced: Nizamuddin's "every community has its own path"
**Front:** What does the Nizamuddin remark express about Chishti outlook?
**Definition (learn):** Nizamuddin's remark that every community has its own path expresses the order's broad outlook of tolerance.
**Breakdown:**
- Chishti association with Hindu and Jain yogis (✅ adv. 10 §1).
- Nizamuddin's remark: every community has its own path (✅ adv. 10 §1).
- Long Delhi career survived dynastic upheavals (✅ adv. 10 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Died 1325 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** MED-2081, MED-2122.
**Trap:** A remark is evidence of outlook, not of universal policy.
**Mains hook:** Use the remark as evidence of tolerance with a source caution.
**Back:** *"Each community, its own path: Nizamuddin's line."*

### Flashcard MED-2124 — Trap: Bhakti did not automatically abolish caste
**Front:** Why is "Bhakti abolished caste" a trap?
**Definition (learn):** Bhakti challenged caste attitudes, but caste survived within many Bhakti communities.
**Breakdown:**
- Caste survived (✅ adv. 10 §5).
- Caste hierarchies persisted within many Bhakti communities (⚠️ owner-flagged variation, owner §8.3).
- Ramanuja opened bhakti to all castes, but later temple authority moderated it (✅ adv. 10 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2093, MED-2114, MED-2098.
**Trap:** "Egalitarian rhetoric" is not social change.
**Mains hook:** Limits of medieval devotional movements in transforming social hierarchy (✅ owner 10 §7).
**Back:** *"Bhakti challenged the caste fence; it didn't tear it down."*

### Flashcard MED-2125 — Trap: Kabir's verses were not textually fixed in his lifetime
**Front:** Why are Kabir's verses a source-criticism trap?
**Definition (learn):** Kabir's message was oral and later written down with additions and alterations; the corpus has layers.
**Breakdown:**
- Oral message written later with additions and alterations (✅ adv. 10 §5).
- Bijak, Granthavali and Adi Granth layers differ (✅ owner 10 §13).
- Later panth formation prevents a modern-reformer reading (✅ owner 10 §8.2 unit 3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2098, MED-2116.
**Trap:** Do not quote a line as verbatim Kabir without a manuscript layer.
**Mains hook:** Source caution as the Mains marker.
**Back:** *"Kabir's words: oral first, manuscript later, with layers."*

### GROUP — 11 Art, Architecture & Culture of the Sultanate

### Flashcard MED-2126 — Sultanate architecture as localisation, not import
**Front:** Why is Sultanate architecture described as localisation rather than the import of foreign forms?
**Definition (learn):** A process in which West Asian and transregional forms were combined with Indian craftsmen, materials and motifs, producing plural regional idioms rather than one imported style.
**Breakdown:**
- Early Sultanate patrons created congregational and political spaces through new work, adaptation and documented reuse (✅ owner 11 §1).
- Arches, vaults and domes had long histories in India and West/central Asia; Sultanate workshops expanded particular arcuate methods (✅ owner 11 §1).
- Indigenous stone-cutters, masons and others were central to construction (✅ owner 11 §1).
- The arch and dome allowed large halls without many pillars and gave an impressive skyline (✅ owner 11 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1206 onward (Aibak) (✅ owner 11 mini-timeline).
**Links:** MED-2127, MED-2131, MED-2167 (Mains thesis), MED-2170 (Indo-Islamic term).
**Trap:** "Indo-Islamic" does not mean foreign forms simply imposed on India; it involved Indian craftsmen, motifs, materials and experiments (✅ owner 11 §5).
**Mains hook:** Discuss Sultanate architecture as synthesis of West Asian forms and Indian building traditions; the answer is plural localisation (✅ owner 11 §8.1).
**Back:** *"Imported forms, Indian hands: localisation, not copying."*

### Flashcard MED-2127 — Arch, dome and lime mortar: the technical transition
**Front:** What technical change did Sultanate workshops bring to Indian building?
**Definition (learn):** The expansion of arcuate geometry, centring and lime-mortar practice enabling wider openings and domed or vaulted spaces, without eliminating post-and-lintel (trabeate) construction.
**Breakdown:**
- Arch + dome: large halls, clear view, skyline; required strong lime mortar (✅ owner 11 §2).
- Fine lime mortar helped the spread of new forms (✅ owner 11 §4).
- Trabeate (post-and-lintel) continued beside arcuate work (✅ owner 11 §8.1; §10).
- The source stresses that arches and domes were known earlier, but the correct scientific method was rare before Sultanate building (✅ adv. 11 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Lime mortar; centring (✅ owner 11 §10).
**Links:** MED-2128 (true arch vs corbelled), MED-2137 (Balban), MED-2138 (Alai Darwaza).
**Trap:** Do not say Turks invented the arch or dome; "Arch and dome were invented by the Turks" is wrong (✅ owner 11 §5).
**Mains hook:** Explain why arch, dome and mortar changed the scale and function of buildings (✅ owner 11 §7).
**Back:** *"Arch, dome, mortar: old tech, new scale, post-and-lintel stays."*

### Flashcard MED-2128 — True arch vs corbelled opening (terminology trap)
**Front:** What separates a true arch from a corbelled opening?
**Definition (learn):** A true arch has radiating wedge-shaped voussoirs carrying compression to supports. A corbelled opening uses projecting horizontal courses that approximate an arch without radial voussoirs.
**Breakdown:**
- True arch: radial voussoirs; thrust carried to piers (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Corbelled opening: horizontal courses project inward; load remains course-by-course (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Arcuate = arch/vault-based construction; "not every pointed opening is structurally true" (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Iwan: not every large portal is an iwan (✅ owner 11 §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2127, MED-2137.
**Trap:** A pointed or arched look does not prove a true arch; check fabric before naming the technique (✅ owner 11 §10).
**Mains hook:** Use the true-arch test to separate visible form from structural mastery.
**Back:** *"Radial wedges make a true arch; courses stacked out make a corbel."*

### Flashcard MED-2129 — Squinch, pendentive and the dome transition
**Front:** What devices carried a dome over a square chamber?
**Definition (learn):** A squinch is a corner device that helps a square chamber receive an octagonal or circular dome zone; a pendentive is a curved triangular transition used only where fabric warrants it.
**Breakdown:**
- Squinch: corner device for square-to-octagon transition (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Pendentive: curved triangular transition; use only with fabric evidence (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Iltutmish's tomb attempts square-to-octagonal/domed form using squinch-like devices (✅ adv. 11 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Iltutmish's tomb (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Links:** MED-2135 (Iltutmish's tomb), MED-2127.
**Trap:** Do not claim a fully intact original dome for Iltutmish's tomb (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Mains hook:** Dome-transition experiments show a technical learning curve rather than a single invention.
**Back:** *"Squinch corners the square; pendentive triangles the dome."*

### Flashcard MED-2130 — Arabesque and Quranic calligraphy as surface programme
**Front:** What are arabesque and calligraphy in Sultanate ornament?
**Definition (learn):** Arabesque is a repeating vegetal or geometric surface organisation; calligraphy is a textual and visual programme in Arabic script that became an art form.
**Breakdown:**
- Arabesque: floral/geometric designs with Quranic inscriptions (✅ adv. 11 §4).
- Quranic calligraphy joins text, patronage, placement and visual rhythm (✅ owner 11 §18).
- Script, date and readability require epigraphic checking (✅ owner 11 §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2131 (Hindu motifs), MED-2132 (Quwwat-ul-Islam).
**Trap:** "Calligraphy was merely textual" is wrong; Arabic script became a decorative art form (✅ adv. 11 §5).
**Mains hook:** Ornament as statecraft and patronage, not only decoration.
**Back:** *"Arabesque spreads the vine; calligraphy writes the faith in art."*

### Flashcard MED-2131 — Indian motifs in Sultanate buildings: bell, lotus, swastika
**Front:** Which Indian motifs appear in Sultanate architecture?
**Definition (learn):** Local workshop motifs such as bell, lotus, swastika, chain, rosette and foliage used alongside arabesque and calligraphy.
**Breakdown:**
- Bell, lotus, swastika and local stone carving were used (✅ owner 11 §5; adv. 11 §4).
- Local lotus, bell, chain, rosette and foliage forms appear in workshop vocabularies (✅ owner 11 §18).
- Figural avoidance was neither absolute nor uniform (✅ owner 11 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2130, MED-2162 (figural avoidance), MED-2163 (craft).
**Trap:** "Early Sultanate buildings used no Indian motifs" is wrong (✅ owner 11 §5).
**Mains hook:** Motifs show localisation within a Persianate idiom.
**Back:** *"Bell, lotus, swastika: local hands on Sultanate walls."*

### Flashcard MED-2132 — Quwwat-ul-Islam (Delhi): reused temple material and phased enlargement
**Front:** What is the layered history of the Quwwat-ul-Islam mosque?
**Definition (learn):** The Delhi mosque beside the Qutb Minar, with an arcaded courtyard of pillars reused from earlier temples and phased enlargements.
**Breakdown:**
- Aibak foundation; Iltutmish and Alauddin enlargements (✅ owner 11 §11).
- Courtyard, prayer screen and reused carved elements (✅ owner 11 §11).
- Inscriptions and fabric document specific reuse; not all material has one origin or meaning (✅ owner 11 §11).
- Reused earlier carved fabric (✅ owner 11 §8.2 unit 1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mosque near Qutb Minar (✅ owner 11 §4).
**Links:** MED-2134 (Qutb Minar), MED-2133 (Ajmer), MED-2168.
**Trap:** Reuse does not mean there was no new architecture (✅ adv. 11 §5).
**Mains hook:** Spolia shows conquest-era rupture, reuse and new construction together.
**Back:** *"Quwwat: temple pillars, Aibak's start, Iltutmish's growth."*

### Flashcard MED-2133 — Arhai Din ka Jhonpra, Ajmer: layered early mosque
**Front:** What is the Arhai Din ka Jhonpra at Ajmer and why is its name a caution?
**Definition (learn):** An early Aibak mosque phase at Ajmer, with Iltutmish enlargement and layered earlier fabric from a Sanskrit college or temple.
**Breakdown:**
- Early Aibak mosque with Iltutmish enlargement (✅ owner 11 §11).
- Layered earlier Sanskrit-college/temple fabric (✅ owner 11 §11).
- Popular name and the "two-and-a-half-day" story are later traditions (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ajmer (✅ owner 11 §4).
**Links:** MED-2132, MED-2126.
**Trap:** Do not accept the "two-and-a-half-day" name as a dated construction fact (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Mains hook:** Use Ajmer as a site-specific case, not an empire-wide formula (✅ owner 11 §1).
**Back:** *"Ajmer's Jhonpra: layered fabric, later name."*

### Flashcard MED-2134 — Qutb Minar: Aibak initiation, Iltutmish completion and later repairs
**Front:** Who began and completed the Qutb Minar and what later changes affected it?
**Definition (learn):** The Qutb Minar was begun by Qutbuddin Aibak and completed by Iltutmish, with later repair and upper alteration, including a Firuz phase.
**Breakdown:**
- Aibak initiated; Iltutmish completed (✅ owner 11 §11; adv. 11 §4).
- Impact from height, tapering form, balconies, red/white sandstone and ribbed projections (✅ owner 11 §1; adv. 11 §1).
- Later repair and upper alteration, including Firuz's phase (✅ owner 11 §11).
- Lightning damage and repair recorded (✅ adv. 11 §4).
- Mini-timeline: Aibak 1206–10; Iltutmish 1211–36 (✅ owner 11 mini-timeline).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1206–10 (Aibak) and 1211–36 (Iltutmish) (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** MED-2132, MED-2135, MED-2164 (name trap), MED-2161 (conservation).
**Trap:** Avoid one-patron attribution; "one monument biography, not one patron" (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Mains hook:** Layered patronage and repair show monument biography as process.
**Back:** *"Aibak starts, Iltutmish finishes, Firuz and lightning touch it."*

### Flashcard MED-2135 — Iltutmish's tomb: square-to-dome experiment
**Front:** What is distinctive about Iltutmish's tomb in Delhi?
**Definition (learn):** A square tomb chamber with inscriptions and arabesque, showing experiments in dome transition from square to octagonal form.
**Breakdown:**
- Square chamber; inscriptions and arabesque (✅ owner 11 §11).
- Experiments in dome transition (✅ owner 11 §11).
- Squinch-like devices in square-to-octagon adaptation (✅ adv. 11 §1).
- Surviving fabric does not justify a simple fully intact original-dome claim (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Iltutmish reign 1211–36 (✅ owner 11 mini-timeline).
**Links:** MED-2129, MED-2136 (Sultan Ghari), MED-2134.
**Trap:** Iltutmish's tomb ≠ Sultan Ghari (Iltutmish's mausoleum for his son).
**Mains hook:** Experiment and loss: the surviving fabric limits the claim.
**Back:** *"Iltutmish's square tomb tries the dome: experiment, not perfection."*

### Flashcard MED-2136 — Sultan Ghari: Iltutmish's mausoleum for his son
**Front:** Whose tomb is Sultan Ghari and why is it a Prelims trap?
**Definition (learn):** Iltutmish's mausoleum for his son Nasiruddin Mahmud, c. 1230s; among the earliest major surviving Delhi Sultanate royal mausolea.
**Breakdown:**
- Built c. 1230s for Nasiruddin Mahmud (✅ owner 11 §11).
- Among the earliest major surviving Delhi Sultanate royal mausolea (✅ owner 11 §11).
- Form is not a standard later tomb (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 1230s (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Links:** MED-2135, MED-2137.
**Trap:** "Sultan Ghari = Iltutmish's own tomb" is wrong; it is the son's mausoleum (✅ owner 11 §24).
**Mains hook:** Royal burial as dynastic memory.
**Back:** *"Sultan Ghari: Iltutmish's son, not Iltutmish."*

### Flashcard MED-2137 — Balban's tomb: first surviving true arch (source marker)
**Front:** What is Balban's tomb's significance in Sultanate architecture?
**Definition (learn):** A late-thirteenth-century ruin that the assigned source uses as the first surviving true-arch marker.
**Breakdown:**
- Source attribution: first surviving true arch (✅ owner 11 §2, §8.2 unit 3; adv. 11 §1).
- Marks structural (not decorative) arch mastery at that point (✅ owner 11 §8.2).
- Isolated example; generalisation of mastery is limited (✅ owner 11 §8.2).
- CAPF 2017 Q50 routes "first extant true arch" to Balban's tomb; competitive-exam discriminator, not UPSC CSE (✅ owner 11 §24).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Late 13th century (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Links:** MED-2128, MED-2138 (Alai Darwaza), MED-2166 (PYQ routes).
**Trap:** "First surviving" is fabric-bounded, not first knowledge of arches in India (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Mains hook:** Use Balban's tomb to show the difference between known arches and mastered construction.
**Back:** *"Balban's tomb: the first true arch that survives, not the first arch ever."*

### Flashcard MED-2138 — Alai Darwaza (1311): mature Khalji arch and dome
**Front:** What makes Alai Darwaza a mature construction and what is the caution?
**Definition (learn):** The 1311 gateway in the Qutb complex, a mature surviving Khalji example of radiating arches, dome transition, proportion and surface ornament.
**Breakdown:**
- Radiating arches, dome transition, proportion and surface ornament (✅ owner 11 §1).
- Red stone and marble (✅ owner 11 §12).
- Mastery of scientific arch and dome by Indian craftsmen (✅ adv. 11 §4).
- Date: 1311 standard date; source places it in Alauddin's building activity (⚠️ owner conflict flagged).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1311 (⚠️ standard date; owner mini-timeline).
**Links:** MED-2137, MED-2139 (Alai Minar), MED-2140 (Siri), MED-2145 (Khalji vs Tughlaq).
**Trap:** Do not call Alai Darwaza the first technically valid arch or dome (✅ owner 11 §1). Calling it "just another gateway" is also wrong (✅ owner 11 §5).
**Mains hook:** Khalji ornament versus Tughlaq austerity (✅ owner 11 §7).
**Back:** *"Alai 1311: radiating arches, dome, mastery, not the first arch."*

### Flashcard MED-2139 — Alai Minar: ambitious incomplete tower
**Front:** What is the Alai Minar and what should not be assumed?
**Definition (learn):** An ambitious Khalji tower project left incomplete beside the Alai Darwaza.
**Breakdown:**
- Ambitious incomplete tower project (✅ owner 11 §12).
- Do not invent a finished form or height (✅ owner 11 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No height is stated in the owner (⚠️ do not state a height).
**Links:** MED-2138.
**Trap:** Do not state the finished height or form of Alai Minar.
**Mains hook:** An incomplete project shows the limit of Khalji ambition and resources.
**Back:** *"Alai Minar: started big, never finished."*

### Flashcard MED-2140 — Siri (Khalji) and Hauz-i-Alai reservoir
**Front:** What are Siri and Hauz-i-Alai in the Khalji landscape?
**Definition (learn):** Siri was a fortified Khalji capital and army-political landscape. Hauz-i-Alai was a reservoir supporting Siri's urban setting, later becoming the Hauz Khas landscape.
**Breakdown:**
- Siri: fortified Khalji capital (✅ owner 11 §12).
- Hauz-i-Alai: reservoir supporting Siri's setting (✅ owner 11 §12).
- Later became the Hauz Khas landscape (✅ owner 11 §12).
- Fragmentary survival limits reconstruction (✅ owner 11 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Khalji (1296–1316 phase per owner mini-timeline) (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** MED-2141 (Hauz Khas complex), MED-2138, MED-2148 (Delhi cities).
**Trap:** Siri = Khalji (✅ owner 11 §24).
**Mains hook:** Water and fortification as urban infrastructure.
**Back:** *"Siri is Khalji; Hauz-i-Alai waters it, then becomes Hauz Khas."*

### Flashcard MED-2141 — Hauz Khas complex: Firuz's repair and madrasa
**Front:** How does the Hauz Khas complex differ from the Alauddin reservoir?
**Definition (learn):** Firuz repaired and reused the reservoir, adding a madrasa and mosque/tomb landscape. It is separate from the Alauddin reservoir (Hauz-i-Alai).
**Breakdown:**
- Firuz repaired/reused reservoir; added madrasa, mosque/tomb landscape (✅ owner 11 §12).
- Hydraulic conservation, education and funerary patronage in one landscape (✅ owner 11 §13).
- Separate Alauddin reservoir from Firuz-period institutional complex (✅ owner 11 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Firuz-period repair (✅ owner 11 §12–13).
**Links:** MED-2140, MED-2144.
**Trap:** Hauz-i-Alai (Alauddin) precedes Firuz's Hauz Khas institutional complex (✅ owner 11 §24).
**Mains hook:** Water, learning and burial in one landscape.
**Back:** *"Alauddin dug the pool; Firuz built the college and tomb around it."*

### Flashcard MED-2142 — Tughlaq style: battered walls, rubble and fortress mass
**Front:** What defines Tughlaq architecture and what is the caution?
**Definition (learn):** Tughlaq buildings used sloping or battered walls, rubble cores, plaster, stone facing and fortress-like mass, but the style is not uniformly austere.
**Breakdown:**
- Sloping/battered walls, rubble cores, restrained surfaces and fortress-like mass in several monuments (✅ owner 11 §1).
- Battered walls give strength and solidity; combined arch and lintel-beam traditions (✅ adv. 11 §1).
- Variation by building, patron and repair history; battered walls, plaster and facing vary (✅ owner 11 §13).
- Economic decline or religious ideology cannot be inferred solely from appearance (✅ owner 11 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1320s — Tughlaqabad and Ghiyasuddin's tomb (✅ owner 11 mini-timeline).
**Links:** MED-2143, MED-2144, MED-2145.
**Trap:** "Tughlaq style equals ornate Khalji style" is wrong; Tughlaq style is more massive, sloping and austere (✅ owner 11 §5).
**Mains hook:** Compare Khalji ornament with Tughlaq austerity; formal contrast alone cannot prove fiscal decline (✅ owner 11 §8.1).
**Back:** *"Tughlaq walls slope and stand; Khalji arches sing."*

### Flashcard MED-2143 — Tughlaqabad, Ghiyasuddin's tomb and Muhammad bin Tughlaq's Jahanpanah and Adilabad
**Front:** Which Tughlaq cities and tombs belong to which patron?
**Definition (learn):** Ghiyasuddin Tughlaq built Tughlaqabad and his tomb. Muhammad bin Tughlaq built Jahanpanah and Adilabad.
**Breakdown:**
- Ghiyasuddin: fort-city, battered walls, rubble/facing, platform and water landscape (✅ owner 11 §13).
- Tughlaqabad: fortress-palace complex with an artificial lake (✅ adv. 11 §4; owner §13).
- Muhammad bin Tughlaq: Jahanpanah and Adilabad, linking dispersed Delhi zones (✅ owner 11 §13).
- Boundaries and functions survive unevenly (✅ owner 11 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1320s (Tughlaqabad and Ghiyasuddin's tomb) (✅ owner 11 mini-timeline).
**Links:** MED-2142, MED-2144, MED-2148.
**Trap:** Jahanpanah/Adilabad = Muhammad Tughlaq; Kotla = Firuz (✅ owner 11 §24).
**Mains hook:** Delhi's dispersed cities as a political-geographic choice.
**Back:** *"Ghiyasuddin walls Tughlaqabad; Muhammad links Jahanpanah and Adilabad."*

### Flashcard MED-2144 — Firoz Shah Kotla / Firozabad (Firuz Shah Tughlaq)
**Front:** What did Firuz Shah Tughlaq build at Kotla and Firozabad?
**Definition (learn):** A palace-fort, congregational mosque and river-oriented city at Firoz Shah Kotla (Firozabad), with a transported Ashokan pillar.
**Breakdown:**
- Palace-fort; congregational mosque; river orientation (✅ owner 11 §13).
- Transported Ashokan pillar (✅ owner 11 §13).
- Hauz Khas repairs and madrasa complex (✅ owner 11 §13).
- Standardisation or economy claims need fabric and source evidence (✅ owner 11 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further in owner.
**Links:** MED-2141, MED-2143, MED-2148.
**Trap:** Kotla = Firuz (✅ owner 11 §24). Do not attribute the Ashokan pillar's original site to Firuz.
**Mains hook:** Reuse of an Ashokan pillar as political display (⚠️ analyst interpretation).
**Back:** *"Firuz's Kotla: river fort, big mosque, Ashoka's pillar carried in."*

### Flashcard MED-2145 — Khalji ornament vs Tughlaq austerity (comparison pair)
**Front:** Compare Khalji and Tughlaq architecture.
**Definition (learn):** Khalji: proportioned arches, ornament and urban ambition (Siri, Alai Darwaza). Tughlaq: batter, mass and austerity (Tughlaqabad, Ghiyasuddin's tomb).
**Breakdown:**
- Khalji: ornate confidence in arches and surface (✅ owner 11 §12; adv. 11 §2).
- Tughlaq: battered walls, rubble cores, fortress mass (✅ owner 11 §2).
- Both use arch principles; Tughlaq combines them with lintel-beam traditions (✅ adv. 11 §1).
- Formal contrast cannot prove fiscal decline, insecurity or ideology (✅ owner 11 §8.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Khalji 1296–1316; Tughlaq 1320s (✅ owner 11 mini-timeline).
**Links:** MED-2138, MED-2142.
**Trap:** The contrast is a style difference, not a moral or fiscal verdict (✅ owner 11 §8.1).
**Mains hook:** Compare ornament and mass with evidence and a limit (✅ owner 11 §8.4).
**Back:** *"Khalji dress, Tughlaq armour: ornament versus mass."*

### Flashcard MED-2146 — Sayyid and Lodi funerary architecture: Muhammad Shah Sayyid, Sikandar Lodi, Bara and Shish Gumbad
**Front:** What are the high-yield Sayyid and Lodi monuments and their plans?
**Definition (learn):** Sayyid and Lodi tombs combined square and octagonal plans, chhatri or kiosk, eave, platform and enclosed-garden relations in varied combinations.
**Breakdown:**
- Muhammad Shah Sayyid's tomb (✅ owner 11 §14).
- Sikandar Lodi's tomb: early mature Indian double-dome and garden-transition case (✅ owner 11 §14).
- Bara Gumbad and Shish Gumbad: conventional names; function and patron not supplied beyond evidence (✅ owner 11 §14).
- Ruler tombs often octagonal and noble tombs square, but this is a tendency (✅ owner 11 §14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sayyid and Lodi phase (✅ owner 11 §14).
**Links:** MED-2147 (building types), MED-2165 (Mughal anticipation).
**Trap:** Avoid a universal "inventor" for the double dome (✅ owner 11 §14).
**Mains hook:** The Lodi phase anticipates Mughal funerary planning without making it inevitable (✅ owner 11 §14).
**Back:** *"Muhammad Sayyid, Sikandar Lodi, Bara and Shish: octagon tends, square nobles."*

### Flashcard MED-2147 — Building types and social functions
**Front:** What social functions did the main building types serve?
**Definition (learn):** The matrix of mosque, tomb, madrasa, khanqah/dargah, fort/palace, water works, sarai and market/workshop quarter.
**Breakdown:**
- Mosque: congregational prayer, sermon, neighbourhood and court identity, sometimes teaching (✅ owner 11 §15).
- Tomb/mausoleum: burial, dynasty or saint memory, pilgrimage and landscape marking (✅ owner 11 §15).
- Madrasa/maktab: learning and manuscript networks (✅ owner 11 §15).
- Khanqah/dargah: Sufi residence and later shrine; doctrine stays in Topic 10 (✅ owner 11 §15).
- Fort/palace: defence, residence, audience, stores, workshops and administration (✅ owner 11 §15).
- Reservoir/canal/baoli/bridge: water security and urban provisioning (✅ owner 11 §15).
- Sarai: travellers, merchants and routes (✅ owner 11 §15).
- Market/workshop quarter: craft, trade and taxation (✅ owner 11 §15).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2146, MED-2148, MED-2163 (craft labour).
**Trap:** Khanqah/dargah doctrine is not this topic's owner; keep Sufi doctrine in MED-2076 onward.
**Mains hook:** Use the building types to show architecture as social infrastructure.
**Back:** *"Mosque prays, tomb remembers, madrasa teaches, fort guards, reservoir waters."*

### Flashcard MED-2148 — Delhi as multiple cities: the hydraulic ladder
**Front:** What is the order of Delhi's shifting urban zones?
**Definition (learn):** Delhi was a shifting urban region, not one continuous city: Mehrauli/Qila Rai Pithora–Qutb zone, Siri with Hauz-i-Alai, Tughlaqabad/Adilabad, Jahanpanah, and Firozabad/Kotla beside the Yamuna.
**Breakdown:**
- Mehrauli / Qila Rai Pithora–Qutb zone (✅ owner 11 §16).
- Siri + Hauz-i-Alai (✅ owner 11 §16).
- Tughlaqabad / Adilabad (✅ owner 11 §16).
- Jahanpanah linking older zones (✅ owner 11 §16).
- Firozabad / Kotla beside the Yamuna (✅ owner 11 §16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ladder only; names and functions can be uncertain (✅ owner 11 §16).
**Links:** MED-2140, MED-2143, MED-2144.
**Trap:** Names and walls can be uncertain because fabric was quarried, repaired or absorbed (✅ owner 11 §16).
**Mains hook:** Water as infrastructure, patronage and landscape (✅ owner 11 §16).
**Back:** *"Mehrauli, Siri, Tughlaqabad, Jahanpanah, Firozabad: five zones, one shifting city."*

### Flashcard MED-2149 — Regional Sultanate architecture: material and form matrix
**Front:** What are the regional architectural material and form signatures in the Sultanate period?
**Definition (learn):** Bengal brick and terracotta; Gujarat fine stone carving; Malwa/Mandu monumental mass; Jaunpur Atala and emphatic portals; Kashmir timber-masonry; Deccan (Gulbarga, Bidar, Mahmud Gawan madrasa).
**Breakdown:**
- Bengal: brick/terracotta, curved cornice/roof responses, Gaur-Pandua monuments (✅ owner 11 §17).
- Gujarat: fine stone carving, screens, pillars, brackets; Ahmedabad and Champaner (✅ owner 11 §17).
- Malwa/Mandu: monumental mass, high platforms, colour/glazed tiles (✅ owner 11 §17).
- Jaunpur: Atala mosque, emphatic central portals; not a derivative copy of Delhi (✅ owner 11 §17).
- Kashmir: timber-masonry, tiered/sloping roofs (✅ owner 11 §17).
- Deccan: Gulbarga, Bidar and Mahmud Gawan madrasa (✅ owner 11 §17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2007, MED-2013, MED-2015, MED-2017, MED-2150.
**Trap:** Regional styles are essential to Sultanate-era regionalisation, not outside it (✅ adv. 11 §5). "Provincial does not mean inferior" (✅ owner 11 §17).
**Mains hook:** Compare Delhi and regional styles as expressions of autonomy (✅ adv. 11 §7).
**Back:** *"Brick Bengal, stone Gujarat, mass Mandu, portal Jaunpur, timber Kashmir."*

### Flashcard MED-2150 — Kashmir timber-masonry and tiered roofs
**Front:** What is distinctive about Kashmir's Sultanate-period building traditions?
**Definition (learn):** Timber-masonry, tiered or sloping roofs and valley material traditions in mosque and shrine landscapes.
**Breakdown:**
- Timber-masonry (✅ owner 11 §17).
- Tiered/sloping roofs (✅ owner 11 §17).
- Climate, material and workshop context; dynasty stays in Topic 08 (✅ owner 11 §17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further in owner.
**Links:** MED-2149, MED-2018.
**Trap:** Kashmir's form is material and climate-driven; do not treat it as a copy of Delhi.
**Mains hook:** Regional material logic shapes form.
**Back:** *"Kashmir builds in wood and stone, roofs that slope to shed snow."*

### Flashcard MED-2151 — Languages of the Sultanate: Persian, Arabic, Sanskrit, Hindavi
**Front:** What were the roles of each major language in the Sultanate?
**Definition (learn):** Persian for chancery, court history, poetry and letters; Arabic for theology, law and scholarship; Sanskrit for scholastic, religious and court continuity; Hindavi and regional languages for oral and performance culture.
**Breakdown:**
- Persian: chancery, court history, poetry, letters; not the only spoken/literary language (✅ owner 11 §19).
- Arabic: theology, law, religious scholarship and transregional learning (✅ owner 11 §19).
- Sanskrit: scholastic, religious and regional-court continuity (✅ owner 11 §19).
- Hindavi and regional languages: oral/literary performance, devotional and everyday communication (✅ owner 11 §19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2152, MED-2158, MED-2159.
**Trap:** "Persian replaced Sanskrit" is not an adequate model (✅ owner 11 §19).
**Mains hook:** Multilingual circulation through courts, scribes, religious centres and performance.
**Back:** *"Persian writes the court, Sanskrit keeps the schools, Hindavi sings the crowd."*

### Flashcard MED-2152 — Hasan Nizami's Taj-ul-Ma'asir and Minhaj's Tabaqat-i Nasiri
**Front:** What are the genres and biases of Hasan Nizami and Minhaj-us-Siraj?
**Definition (learn):** Hasan Nizami's Taj-ul-Ma'asir is an ornate conquest and court chronicle; Minhaj-us-Siraj's Tabaqat-i Nasiri is a dynastic and biographical history.
**Breakdown:**
- Hasan Nizami, Taj-ul-Ma'asir: ornate conquest/court chronicle; panegyric and selective (✅ owner 11 §19).
- Minhaj-us-Siraj, Tabaqat-i Nasiri: dynastic/biographical history; clerical-court and gendered elite gaze (✅ owner 11 §19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2151, MED-2155 (Barani), MED-2156 (Isami).
**Trap:** Do not treat chronicles as neutral; both are elite, panegyric or selective sources (✅ owner 11 §19).
**Mains hook:** Source criticism with genre, patronage and gaze.
**Back:** *"Nizami praises the conquest; Minhaj builds the dynasty list."*

### Flashcard MED-2153 — Amir Khusrau: poetry, history and musical memory
**Front:** Why is Amir Khusrau central to Sultanate literary and musical memory?
**Definition (learn):** Amir Khusrau is an author of Persian poetry, panegyric, history and court observation, and central to Persian-Hindavi literary and musical memory.
**Breakdown:**
- Persian poetry, panegyric, history, language and court observation (✅ owner 11 §19).
- Central to Persian-Hindavi literary and musical memory (✅ owner 11 §21).
- Later traditions credit him with qawwali, sitar, tabla, khayal and others; no single-person invention claim without evidence (✅ owner 11 §21).
- Cultural interaction illustrated by his reputation (✅ owner 11 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None in owner.
**Links:** MED-2154 (qawwali and instruments), MED-2151.
**Trap:** Amir Khusrau is not safely the sole inventor of tabla, sitar or qawwali (✅ owner 11 §24).
**Mains hook:** Use Khusrau as a case of later legend versus contemporary evidence.
**Back:** *"Khusrau: poet, panegyrist, musician in memory, not the sole inventor."*

### Flashcard MED-2154 — Qawwali, sama and the instrument-invention caution
**Front:** What does the owner say about qawwali, sama and instrument invention?
**Definition (learn):** Sufi sama and the developing qawwali genealogy belong to khanqah, court and vernacular interaction, with internal religious debate. Later invention credits require contemporary evidence.
**Breakdown:**
- Sama and the qawwali genealogy: khanqah, court and vernacular interaction (✅ owner 11 §21).
- Internal religious debate over sama (✅ owner 11 §21; MED-2087).
- Court music, devotional song and popular performance overlapped with different patrons and audiences (✅ owner 11 §21).
- Single-person invention claims need contemporary evidence (✅ owner 11 §21).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None in owner.
**Links:** MED-2087, MED-2153.
**Trap:** Do not assign qawwali, sitar, tabla to one inventor (✅ owner 11 §21).
**Mains hook:** Music as cultural production with internal debate.
**Back:** *"Sama debated, qawwali grows, Khusrau credited: check the evidence."*

### Flashcard MED-2155 — Ziauddin Barani: normative history and aristocratic bias
**Front:** What is Ziauddin Barani's genre and limit?
**Definition (learn):** Barani wrote history and normative political thought with an aristocratic and didactic bias.
**Breakdown:**
- History and normative political thought (✅ owner 11 §19).
- Aristocratic and didactic bias (✅ owner 11 §19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None in owner.
**Links:** MED-2152, MED-2157 (Shams-i Siraj Afif).
**Trap:** Barani's normative ideals are not a description of actual practice.
**Mains hook:** Separate political ideals from court practice.
**Back:** *"Barani preaches the ideal of the ruler; test it against practice."*

### Flashcard MED-2156 — Isami's Futuh-us-Salatin
**Front:** What is Isami's Futuh-us-Salatin and what perspective does it carry?
**Definition (learn):** A Persian verse history with a Deccan and displacement perspective.
**Breakdown:**
- Persian verse history (✅ owner 11 §19).
- Deccan and displacement perspective (✅ owner 11 §19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None in owner.
**Links:** MED-2152, MED-2157.
**Trap:** Do not treat the verse history as a neutral political narrative.
**Mains hook:** Displacement perspectives in Sultanate historiography.
**Back:** *"Isami's verse: a Deccan view of displacement."*

### Flashcard MED-2157 — Shams-i Siraj Afif and Firuz-period memory
**Front:** What is Shams-i Siraj Afif's perspective on Firuz's reign?
**Definition (learn):** A Firuz-period or later memory and public-work narrative with retrospective admiration.
**Breakdown:**
- Firuz-period/later memory; public-work narrative (✅ owner 11 §19).
- Retrospective admiration (✅ owner 11 §19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None in owner.
**Links:** MED-2144 (Firuz), MED-2155.
**Trap:** Retrospective admiration is not contemporaneous evidence.
**Mains hook:** Public works as ideology and memory.
**Back:** *"Afif remembers Firuz's public works with admiration."*

### Flashcard MED-2158 — Education and knowledge circulation: maktabs to madrasas
**Front:** Which institutions supported learning and book circulation in the Sultanate?
**Definition (learn):** Maktabs, madrasas, mosques, khanqahs, libraries and private teachers for Arabic and Persian literacy; Sanskrit pathshalas, temples, mathas and regional courts for Sanskrit learning.
**Breakdown:**
- Maktabs, madrasas, mosques, khanqahs, libraries and private teachers (✅ owner 11 §20).
- Sanskrit pathshalas, temples, mathas and regional courts (✅ owner 11 §20).
- Paper-making, copying, calligraphy, binding and patronage moved texts (✅ owner 11 §20).
- Madrasa curricula varied; no single curriculum applies everywhere (✅ owner 11 §20).
- Literacy and manuscript access remained socially unequal (✅ owner 11 §20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2151, MED-2147.
**Trap:** Do not project one curriculum across all madrasas.
**Mains hook:** Knowledge circulation shows social inequality.
**Back:** *"Maktab for letters, madrasa for law, pathshala for Sanskrit: many routes, unequal access."*

### Flashcard MED-2159 — Terminology: Indo-Islamic architecture (broad convenience)
**Front:** What does "Indo-Islamic architecture" mean and what must it not imply?
**Definition (learn):** A broad convenience for buildings produced under Islamic patrons in Indian environments through transregional forms, Indian materials, craftsmen and workshop practices.
**Breakdown:**
- Transregional forms plus Indian materials and craftsmen (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Must not imply one Sultanate style, a clean imported/indigenous division or seamless Hindu-Muslim synthesis (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Synthesis was organic and varied by region (⚠️ owner 11 §8.3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2126, MED-2149.
**Trap:** "Indo-Islamic" ≠ mechanical Hindu + Muslim addition (✅ owner 11 §8.3).
**Mains hook:** A terminology answer must mark the convenience.
**Back:** *"Indo-Islamic: a convenience label, not a seamless style."*

### Flashcard MED-2160 — Structural and architectural vocabulary: dome, mihrab, minar, iwan, batter
**Front:** Which architectural terms must be used precisely in answers?
**Definition (learn):** Trabeate, arcuate, true arch, corbelled opening, dome, squinch, pendentive, minar, mihrab, iwan, courtyard mosque, rubble masonry, lime mortar, calligraphy, arabesque and batter.
**Breakdown:**
- Trabeate: post-and-lintel load path (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Arcuate: arch/vault-based; true arch with radial voussoirs (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Minar: tower with ritual, landmark and political possibilities (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Mihrab: prayer-direction niche (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Iwan: vaulted hall/open-fronted bay (✅ owner 11 §10).
- Batter: inward-sloping wall face (✅ owner 11 §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2127, MED-2128, MED-2129, MED-2142.
**Trap:** Batter is not uniquely Egyptian or uniform across Tughlaq work (✅ owner 11 §10).
**Mains hook:** Precise vocabulary as a marker of answer quality.
**Back:** *"Trabeate beams, arcuate arches, minar ticks, mihrab faces Mecca, batter slopes."*

### Flashcard MED-2161 — Conservation: Qutb Minar's repair and authenticity
**Front:** What does conservation of the Qutb complex require us to distinguish?
**Definition (learn):** Restored fabric must be distinguished from original phases; UNESCO's description supports present authenticity, integrity, ASI management and reversible repair, not medieval motives.
**Breakdown:**
- Qutb Minar records construction, lightning/earthquake damage, repair and later alteration (✅ owner 11 §23).
- UNESCO's Qutb property description supports present authenticity and reversible repair (✅ owner 11 §23; ⚠️ not fetched in this session).
- Compatible materials, minimum intervention, documentation and reversibility (✅ owner 11 §23).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2134, MED-2164.
**Trap:** Present conservation is not medieval provenance (✅ owner 11 §9).
**Mains hook:** Conservation as governance, not as medieval evidence.
**Back:** *"Repair is not the original; document, minimal, reversible."*

### Flashcard MED-2162 — Figural imagery: constrained, not absent
**Front:** What is the owner's position on figural avoidance in Sultanate art?
**Definition (learn):** Figural avoidance was constrained in many religious settings but not absolute; figural imagery survived in courtly and private objects, textiles, manuscripts and regional contexts.
**Breakdown:**
- Figural imagery constrained in many religious settings (✅ owner 11 §18).
- Survived in courtly/private objects, textiles, manuscripts and regional contexts (✅ owner 11 §18; ⚠️ owner §8.3).
- Absence of a large Sultanate painting corpus does not prove absence of painting (✅ owner 11 §18).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2131, MED-2163.
**Trap:** "Figural avoidance was absolute" is wrong (✅ owner 11 §1).
**Mains hook:** Survival bias shapes what we can see.
**Back:** *"Figures are constrained in shrines, not absent everywhere."*

### Flashcard MED-2163 — Non-elite craft labour and material supply
**Front:** Who built the Sultanate's cultural field and how were materials supplied?
**Definition (learn):** Stone-cutters, masons, brickmakers, lime burners, carpenters, calligraphers, tile workers, transporters and labourers produced the field; quarrying, reused blocks, brick, timber, lime, fuel and transport linked monuments to countryside and labour.
**Breakdown:**
- Designs depended on architects, master masons, stonecutters, brickmakers and lime burners (✅ owner 11 §15).
- Workshops made structural and material decisions (✅ owner 11 §15).
- Quarrying, reused blocks, brick, timber, lime, plaster, water, fuel and transport (✅ owner 11 §15).
- Non-elite masons, artisans, carriers and workshop labourers produced the field (✅ owner 11 §22).
- Exact guild or wage claims require evidence (✅ owner 11 §15).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2147, MED-2131.
**Trap:** "Indian craftsmanship" as a faceless input is wrong (✅ owner 11 §15).
**Mains hook:** Localisation is a social process, not a seamless celebration (✅ owner 11 §22).
**Back:** *"The masons build; the lime burners and carriers hold the wall up."*

### Flashcard MED-2164 — Trap: Qutb Minar and the saint's name
**Front:** Why should Qutb Minar not be linked mechanically to the saint?
**Definition (learn):** The tower was not originally called Qutb Minar in the way later tradition imagined; avoid linking it mechanically to the saint.
**Breakdown:**
- Name not original in the sense later tradition assumed (✅ owner 11 §4).
- Mechanical link to the saint should be avoided (✅ owner 11 §4).
- Ritual, commemorative and political meanings are debated (✅ owner 11 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2134, MED-2161.
**Trap:** A name is not a dated construction fact.
**Mains hook:** Monument biography requires phase-by-phase evidence.
**Back:** *"Qutb's name is later story, not the builder's record."*

### Flashcard MED-2165 — Lodi phase and Mughal funerary planning (anticipation without inevitability)
**Front:** How should the Lodi phase be linked to later Mughal architecture?
**Definition (learn):** Lodi funerary architecture anticipates Mughal planning; it does not make Mughal architecture inevitable.
**Breakdown:**
- Lodi plans and enclosed gardens anticipate Mughal forms (✅ owner 11 §14).
- Inevitability is not claimed (✅ owner 11 §14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2146.
**Trap:** Do not present Lodi architecture as the sole ancestor of Mughal tombs.
**Mains hook:** Continuity as a bounded claim.
**Back:** *"Lodi anticipates, Mughal is not inevitable."*

### Flashcard MED-2166 — PYQ routes for Sultanate architecture
**Front:** Which routes are relevant, and what is the status of PYQ coverage for this topic?
**Definition (learn):** The repository routes zero direct UPSC Civil Services PYQs to this topic for 2018–2026. Adjacent routes are 2018 GS-I Q1 (heritage conservation) and CAPF 2017 Q50 (first extant true arch).
**Breakdown:**
- Zero direct UPSC CSE PYQs (✅ owner 11 §24).
- 2018 GS-I Q1 (safeguarding Indian art heritage): adjacent; Heritage Conservation owner (✅ owner 11 §24).
- CAPF 2017 Q50: competitive-exam discriminator; local OCR answer Balban's tomb (✅ owner 11 §24).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2137, MED-2161.
**Trap:** Do not invent UPSC questions for this topic (✅ owner 11 §24).
**Mains hook:** Use the adjacent 2018 Q1 route for heritage conservation.
**Back:** *"No direct PYQ; CAPF 2017 Q50 names Balban's tomb as the first true arch."*

### Flashcard MED-2167 — Mains thesis: Sultanate architecture (10/15/20 marks)
**Front:** How should a Sultanate architecture answer be built at 10, 15 and 20 marks?
**Definition (learn):** Executable scaffolds from the owner's answer architecture.
**Breakdown:**
- 10 marks: synthesis thesis, Qutb Minar and Alai Darwaza; conclusion: new idiom, not import (✅ owner 11 §8.4).
- 15 marks: add Tughlaq contrast and regional variation; conclusion: plural, not one style (✅ owner 11 §8.4).
- 20 marks: five-unit integration; regional styles; historiographical note (✅ owner 11 §8.4; §25).
- Thesis: technical change without eliminating post-and-lintel (✅ owner 11 §8.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 marks/150 words; 15 and 20 marks/250 words (✅ owner 11 §25).
**Links:** MED-2126, MED-2145, MED-2149.
**Trap:** Do not present the answer as a single style; avoid one-patron attribution.
**Mains hook:** Localisation not binary.
**Back:** *"Ten: synthesis and two monuments. Twenty: process, regions, sources, verdict."*

### Flashcard MED-2168 — Advanced: conflict and rapprochement in stone
**Front:** How does Satish Chandra frame conflict and rapprochement in Sultanate buildings?
**Definition (learn):** Early temple conversion and spolia show conquest and reuse; later architecture shows collaboration between West Asian forms and Indian craftsmen.
**Breakdown:**
- Quwwat-ul-Islam's arcaded courtyard used pillars from earlier temples (✅ adv. 11 §1).
- Turkish rulers used Indian craftsmen and later West Asian master architects (✅ adv. 11 §1).
- Technical and aesthetic fusion (✅ adv. 11 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2132, MED-2126.
**Trap:** Spolia does not mean there was no new architecture (✅ adv. 11 §5).
**Mains hook:** Discuss "born from both rupture and synthesis" with Quwwat-ul-Islam, Qutb Minar and Alai Darwaza (✅ adv. 11 §7).
**Back:** *"Conflict in stone, collaboration in arches: both at once."*

### Flashcard MED-2169 — Advanced: Indian builders and the arch — the rarity of scientific method
**Front:** Did Indian builders know the arch before the Sultanate?
**Definition (learn):** They knew arches and domes, but large-scale scientific use was rare before Sultanate expansion.
**Breakdown:**
- Arch and dome were known in India earlier (✅ adv. 11 §1).
- Correct scientific method was rare; Sultanate building made the arcuate method widespread (✅ adv. 11 §1).
- Balban's tomb as the first surviving true arch in the source (✅ adv. 11 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2127, MED-2137.
**Trap:** "Indian builders did not know arches at all" is wrong (✅ adv. 11 §5).
**Mains hook:** Diffusion versus mastery.
**Back:** *"Known, but rarely mastered: the Sultanate made it common."*

### Flashcard MED-2170 — Advanced: Tughlaq arch and lintel combination
**Front:** How did Tughlaq builders combine arch and lintel traditions?
**Definition (learn):** Tughlaq architecture used batter for strength and combined arch principles with lintel-and-beam traditions.
**Breakdown:**
- Batter for strength and solidity (✅ adv. 11 §1).
- Arch principles with lintel-and-beam traditions (✅ adv. 11 §1).
- Restrained decoration varied by building (✅ owner 11 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2142, MED-2160.
**Trap:** Tughlaq style is not a single fixed recipe.
**Mains hook:** Technical combination as an example of localisation.
**Back:** *"Tughlaq walls slope; lintels still hold the roof."*

### Flashcard MED-2171 — Advanced: Hauz-i-Alai and Firuz's hydraulic works
**Front:** How do Hauz Shamsi, Hauz-i-Alai and Firuz's works show water as infrastructure?
**Definition (learn):** Hauz Shamsi, Hauz-i-Alai and Hauz Khas show that water was infrastructure, patronage and landscape in Delhi.
**Breakdown:**
- Hauz Shamsi (✅ owner 11 §16).
- Hauz-i-Alai and Hauz Khas (✅ owner 11 §16).
- Firuz's hydraulic works (✅ owner 11 §16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2140, MED-2141, MED-2148.
**Trap:** Water works are not only ornamental.
**Mains hook:** Water as urban infrastructure.
**Back:** *"Water is infrastructure, patronage and landscape at once."*

### Flashcard MED-2172 — Advanced: Khalji architecture and urban-hydraulic programmes
**Front:** How did Khalji architecture join fortification, water and ceremony?
**Definition (learn):** Siri, Alai Darwaza, Alai Minar and Hauz-i-Alai joined fortification, water, ceremony, learning and monumental visibility.
**Breakdown:**
- Fortification: Siri (✅ owner 11 §12).
- Water: Hauz-i-Alai (✅ owner 11 §12).
- Ceremony: Alai Darwaza (✅ owner 11 §12).
- "Ornate confidence" is interpretation, not a fabric fact (✅ owner 11 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2138, MED-2140.
**Trap:** Interpretation vs fabric fact.
**Mains hook:** Use the Khalji programme to argue for urban ambition.
**Back:** *"Khalji joins walls, water and ceremony: interpretation, not fact alone."*

### Flashcard MED-2173 — Advanced: Sultanate painting and manuscripts
**Front:** Does the absence of a large Sultanate painting corpus prove absence of painting?
**Definition (learn):** No. Painting and illustrated manuscripts survive less evenly than stone buildings; Jain, regional, court and workshop traditions intersected.
**Breakdown:**
- Painting survives less evenly than stone buildings (✅ owner 11 §18).
- Jain, regional, court and workshop traditions intersected (✅ owner 11 §18).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2162, MED-2149.
**Trap:** Survival bias.
**Mains hook:** Source survival shapes the visible history.
**Back:** *"No big corpus is not no painting: survival is uneven."*

### Flashcard MED-2174 — Source ladder for the Sultanate architecture unit
**Front:** Which evidence types are secure and what limits each?
**Definition (learn):** Dated inscriptions, stratigraphy, structural fabric, spolia, chronicles, manuscripts and conservation records, each with its own limit.
**Breakdown:**
- Dated foundation/repair inscriptions: patron, date, reuse claim; formula and relocation (✅ owner 11 §9).
- Archaeological stratigraphy: partial excavation (✅ owner 11 §9).
- Structural fabric: visible technique does not prove motive (✅ owner 11 §9).
- Spolia: appropriation, economy and adaptation need site-specific analysis (✅ owner 11 §9).
- Chronicles and court poetry: panegyric and elite gaze (✅ owner 11 §9).
- Manuscripts and objects: attribution uneven (✅ owner 11 §9).
- Conservation records: present conservation is not medieval provenance (✅ owner 11 §9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None further.
**Links:** MED-2161, MED-2152.
**Trap:** Separate inscriptional attribution, archaeological phase, chronicler claim, stylistic inference and later tradition (✅ owner 11 §9).
**Mains hook:** Method: evidence claim, date, genre, limit.
**Back:** *"Inscription dates, fabric shows, chronicles praise, conservation repairs."*

### GROUP — 12 Babur & the Central Asian Backdrop

### Flashcard MED-2175 — Central Asian backdrop: narrowing options, not destiny
**Front:** Why did Central Asian instability push Babur toward India, and what is the correct causal language?
**Definition (learn):** Uzbek pressure and Timurid fragmentation narrowed Babur's options; Kabul's limits and Punjab's opening made Hindustan a viable base. The move was strategic choice, not destiny.
**Breakdown:**
- Timurid princes fought each other, letting the Uzbeks (Trans-Oxiana) and Safavids (Iran) rise (✅ owner 12 §1).
- Babur twice won Samarqand but could not hold it; after Uzbek pressure he shifted to Kabul in 1504 (✅ owner 12 §1, §8.2).
- India was attractive: Kabul's income was too small for army and nobles, Punjab had Timurid claims, India could be a refuge (✅ owner 12 §1).
- The correct causal language: "narrowing options plus strategic choice, not Central Asian destiny" (✅ owner 12 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1494 Farghana; 1504 Kabul (✅ owner 12 mini-timeline).
**Links:** MED-2176, MED-2177, MED-2178, MED-2182, MED-2215.
**Trap:** Babur did not come only because Indian nobles invited him; he had strategic, financial and Timurid motives (✅ owner 12 §5).
**Mains hook:** "Babur's Indian empire was born from a Central Asian failure but sustained by Indian resources" — discuss (✅ adv. 12 §7).
**Back:** *"Uzbeks narrow the door; Kabul is the step; India is the choice."*

### Flashcard MED-2176 — Timur's death (1405) and Timurid fragmentation
**Front:** What happened to the Timurid world after Timur, and what did it allow?
**Definition (learn):** After Timur's death (1405) and Shahrukh, Timur's empire fragmented among successors; Samarkand and Herat kept great prestige and value.
**Breakdown:**
- Timur dies 1405; Timurid power later fragments (✅ owner 12 mini-timeline).
- Timurid princes' infighting let the Uzbeks and Safavids rise (✅ owner 12 §1).
- Samarkand and Herat retained cultural-economic value (✅ owner 12 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1405 (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** MED-2175, MED-2178, MED-2179.
**Trap:** Timurid prestige is not Timurid unity; Babur's claim rested on lineage, not on a surviving state.
**Mains hook:** Fragmentation as the background to Babur's Central Asian failure.
**Back:** *"Fourteen-oh-five: Timur dies; Timurids quarrel; Uzbeks rise."*

### Flashcard MED-2177 — Babur inherits Farghana (1494), aged twelve
**Front:** When and how did Babur begin his political career?
**Definition (learn):** Babur succeeded to Farghana in 1494 at the age of twelve.
**Breakdown:**
- 1494 at twelve (✅ owner 12 mini-timeline).
- Farghana was the base from which he repeatedly captured and lost Samarkand (✅ owner 12 §12).
- Paternal Timurid genealogy (✅ owner 12 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1494 (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** MED-2178, MED-2202 (genealogy).
**Trap:** Do not place his Farghana rule after Kabul; Farghana came first (✅ owner 12 §12).
**Mains hook:** Early loss of a home base shaped his later choice.
**Back:** *"Ninety-four, Farghana, age twelve: a throne too small."*

### Flashcard MED-2178 — Samarkand: twice won, twice lost
**Front:** What was the Samarkand struggle and why did Babur move to Kabul?
**Definition (learn):** Babur won Samarkand twice but could not hold it against Uzbek pressure; he then shifted to Kabul (1504).
**Breakdown:**
- Twice won; twice lost (✅ owner 12 §1, §8.2).
- Shaibani Khan ousted Babur from Samarkand (✅ owner 12 §2).
- Shift to Kabul in 1504 (✅ owner 12 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kabul 1504 (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** MED-2175, MED-2179, MED-2182.
**Trap:** Kabul is not a substitute for Samarkand in prestige; it was a fallback (✅ owner 12 §13).
**Mains hook:** A failed Central Asian project as a driver of an Indian turn.
**Back:** *"Samarkand won, lost, won, lost: Kabul is the fallback."*

### Flashcard MED-2179 — Muhammad Shaybani Khan and Uzbek consolidation
**Front:** Who were the Uzbeks under Shaybani and what did they achieve?
**Definition (learn):** Muhammad Shaybani Khan's Uzbek consolidation, which dominated Trans-Oxiana and narrowed Babur's options.
**Breakdown:**
- Uzbek pressure and weak Timurid cooperation narrowed options (✅ owner 12 §12).
- Shaibani Khan ousted Babur from Samarkand (✅ owner 12 §2).
- Shah Ismail defeated and killed Shaibani Khan near Merv in 1510 (✅ owner 12 mini-timeline).
- Ottoman victory over Shah Ismail in 1514 left Uzbeks dominant in Trans-Oxiana (✅ owner 12 mini-timeline).
